.
Combining this with the compatibility of these isomorphisms with the descent data defined by the

{\mathcal{O}}_{F,(p),+}^{\times}
-actions, we obtain analogous results for the vector bundles on
Y_{U^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
and
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}

with respect to the morphism
\rho_{g}:Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to Y_{U^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
.

Summing up, we have now proved the following:

Theorem 3.1.2
.

Letting
{\mathcal{C}}
denote the conormal bundle of the closed immersion

h:Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}\hookrightarrow Y_{U}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}Y_{U}
,
there exists a decomposition

{{\mathcal{C}}}=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{0}}{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\tau}
,
together with an increasing filtration

0={\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{0}({{\mathcal{C}}}_{\tau})\subset{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{1}({{\mathcal{C}}}_{\tau})\subset\cdots\subset{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}({{\mathcal{C}}}_{\tau})\subset{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}}({{\mathcal{C}}}_{\tau})={{\mathcal{C}}}_{\tau}

for each
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
, such that

\mathcal{D}_{\theta}\cong{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j}({{\mathcal{C}}}_{\tau})

for each
j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
.
Furthermore if
g^{-1}Ug\subset U^{\prime}
, then the canonical isomorphism
\rho_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{C}}
restricts to

\rho_{g}^{*}{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\tau})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{\tau})

for all
\tau
and
j
as above, and the resulting diagram

\textstyle{{\rho}_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\rho}_{g}^{*}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j}({{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{\tau})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j}({{\mathcal{C}}}_{\tau})}

commutes.

Corollary 3.1.3
.

If
U
is sufficiently small that Proposition
2.4.1
holds, then
there is an isomorphism

(7)

\pi_{1}^{*}\delta^{-1}\otimes\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\bigwedge^{d}{\mathcal{C}},

where
{\mathcal{C}}
is the conormal bundle of the closed immersion
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2}):Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}\hookrightarrow Y_{U}\times Y_{U}
.
Furthermore the isomorphisms are compatible with the action of
g\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
, in the sense that if

U^{\prime}
is also sufficiently small and
g^{-1}Ug\subset U^{\prime}
, then the resulting diagram

\textstyle{\rho_{g}^{*}(\pi_{1}^{\prime*}\delta^{\prime-1}\otimes\pi_{1}^{\prime*}\omega^{\prime}\otimes\pi_{2}^{\prime*}\omega^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\rho_{g}^{*}\bigwedge^{d}{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}^{*}\rho_{g}^{*}\delta^{\prime-1}\otimes\pi_{1}^{*}\rho_{g}^{*}\omega^{\prime}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\rho_{g}^{*}\omega^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\bigwedge^{d}\rho_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}^{*}\delta^{-1}\otimes\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\bigwedge^{d}{\mathcal{C}}}

commutes.

Remark 3.1.4
.

We comment briefly on the lack of symmetry between the two degeneracy maps
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
in
the statements. Exchanging
\widetilde{\pi}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\pi}_{2}
in the definition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}

gives the line bundle

\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\theta}:=\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{-1}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}=\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}^{-1}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}

on
Y
, descending to a line bundle
{\mathcal{E}}_{\theta}
on
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
. We may then use the isomorphism

\theta(\varpi_{\mathfrak{P}})^{-1}(\wedge^{2}\psi_{\theta}^{*}):\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}

to define an isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\theta}
compatible with descent data,
and hence inducing an isomorphism
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}\cong{\mathcal{E}}_{\theta}
. The isomorphisms are furthermore compatible with the
action of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
, but are dependent (up to an element of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
) on the choices of
\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}
. There is
however a canonical isomorphism

\pi_{2}^{*}\delta\,\,\cong\,\,\mathrm{Nm}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{P}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\pi_{1}^{*}\delta\,\,\cong\,\,\pi_{1}^{*}\delta

making
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
interchangeable in the statement of Corollary
3.1.3
.

3.2.
Dualizing sheaves

We now combine the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism over
Y_{U}
with the
description of the conormal bundle obtained in the preceding section in order
to establish a Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism over
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
.

Recall that the Kodaira–Spencer filtration on
\Omega^{1}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}
yields an isomorphism

\wedge^{d}\Omega^{1}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}\cong\delta^{-1}\otimes\omega^{\otimes 2}

(see
[
RX17
, §2.8]
and
[
Dia23
, Thm. 3.3.1]
), which is furthermore compatible
with the action of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
in the same sense as the isomorphism of
Corollary
3.1.3
. The identification

\Omega^{1}_{(Y_{U}\times_{{\mathcal{O}}}Y_{U})/{\mathcal{O}}}=p_{1}^{*}\Omega^{1}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U}\times_{\mathcal{O}}Y_{U}}}p_{2}^{*}\Omega^{1}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}
,
where
p_{1},p_{2}:Y_{U}\times_{\mathcal{O}}Y_{U}\to Y_{U}
are the projection maps, therefore yields an isomorphism

(8)

\wedge^{2d}\Omega^{1}_{(Y_{U}\times_{\mathcal{O}}Y_{U})/{\mathcal{O}}}\cong p_{1}^{*}(\delta^{-1}\otimes\omega^{\otimes 2})\otimes p_{2}^{*}(\delta^{-1}\otimes\omega^{\otimes 2}).

We recall a few general facts about dualizing sheaves (see for example
[
StaX
, Ch. 0DWE]
).
If a scheme
Y
is Cohen–Macaulay (of constant dimension) over a base
R
, then it admits a relative
dualizing sheaf
{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/R}
, which is invertible if and only if
Y
is Gorenstein over
R
. If
\rho:Y\to Y^{\prime}
is étale
and
Y^{\prime}
is Cohen–Macaulay over
R
, then
{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/R}
is canonically identified with
\rho^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y^{\prime}/R}
, and
with
\wedge_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}^{n}\Omega^{1}_{Y/R}
if
Y
is smooth over
R
of dimension
n
. More generally if

X
is smooth over
R
of dimension
n
,
Y
is syntomic over
R
of dimension
n-d
, and
i:Y\hookrightarrow X

is a closed immersion with conormal bundle
{\mathcal{C}}_{Y/X}
, then

(9)

{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/R}\,\,\cong\,\,(\wedge_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}^{d}{\mathcal{C}}_{Y/X})^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}i^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{X/R}\,\,\cong\,\,(\wedge_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}^{d}{\mathcal{C}}_{Y/X})^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}i^{*}(\wedge^{n}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}\Omega^{1}_{X/R}).

Furthermore the isomorphism is compatible in the obvious sense with étale base-change
X\to X^{\prime}
, and if
Y
itself is
smooth over
R
, then it may be identified with the isomorphism arising from the canonical exact sequence

(10)

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{Y/X}\longrightarrow i^{*}\Omega^{1}_{X/R}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{Y/R}\longrightarrow 0.

Combining the isomorphisms (
7
), (
8
) and
9
), we conclude that
if
U
is sufficiently small that Proposition
2.4.1
holds, then we have an isomorphism

(11)

{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}\,\,\cong\,\,\pi_{2}^{*}\delta^{-1}\otimes\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\omega.

Furthermore the compatibility of (
7
) and (
8
) with the action of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
and that
of (
9
) with étale base-change implies that if
U^{\prime}
is also sufficiently small for Proposition
2.4.1

to hold and
g\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
is such that
g^{-1}Ug\subset U^{\prime}
, then the diagram

(12)

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\rho_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}&\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}&\pi_{2}^{*}\rho_{g}^{*}\delta^{\prime-1}\otimes\pi_{1}^{*}\rho_{g}^{*}\omega^{\prime}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\rho_{g}^{*}\omega^{\prime}\\
\wr\downarrow&&\downarrow\wr\\
{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}&\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}&\pi_{2}^{*}\delta^{-1}\otimes\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\end{array}

commutes, where we have written
\rho_{g}
for both morphisms
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to Y_{U_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{P}})}
and
Y_{U}\to Y_{U^{\prime}}
as usual, the top arrow is the pull-back
of (
11
) for
U^{\prime}
, and the right arrow is induced
by the isomorphisms
\rho_{g}^{*}\delta^{\prime}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\delta
and
\rho_{g}^{*}\omega^{\prime}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\omega
defined in
§
2.3
.

Note that we may therefore remove the assumption that
h:Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to Y_{U}\times Y_{U}
is a closed immersion. Indeed for any
U^{\prime\prime}
sufficiently small ,
we may choose
U
normal in
U^{\prime\prime}
so that Proposition
2.4.1
holds, and the commutativity of (
12
) for
g\in U^{\prime\prime}
and
U=U^{\prime}

implies that (
11
) descends to such an isomorphism over
Y_{U^{\prime\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
, which is furthermore compatible with the action of

\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
in the usual sense. We have now proved:

Theorem 3.2.1
.

If
U
is a sufficiently small open compact subgroup of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
containing
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F,p})
,
then there is an isomorphism

{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}\,\,\cong\,\,\pi_{2}^{*}\delta^{-1}\otimes\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\omega;

furthermore the isomorphisms for varying
U
are compatible with the action of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
in the sense that
diagram (
12
) commutes.

Remark 3.2.2
.

Recall also from Remark
3.1.4
that
\pi_{1}^{*}\delta\cong\pi_{2}^{*}\delta
, so that we may exchange
\pi_{1}
and

\pi_{2}
in the statement of the theorem, and deduce also the existence of isomorphisms

\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}\,\,\cong\,\,{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes 2},

compatible in the obvious sense with the action
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
.

We will also need to relate the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphisms at level
U
and level
U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
.
Consider the morphisms

\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}:\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*\vee}:\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{-1}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{-1}

induced by the universal isogeny
\psi
over
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
. Tensoring over all
\theta\in\Sigma

then yields morphisms which descend to define

{\pi}_{2}^{*}\omega\longrightarrow{\pi}_{1}^{*}\omega\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\pi}_{1}^{*}(\delta^{-1}\otimes\omega)\longrightarrow\pi_{2}^{*}(\delta^{-1}\otimes\omega).

Combining these with (
11
) and the isomorphisms

\pi_{j}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}\cong\pi_{j}^{*}(\delta^{-1}\otimes\omega)\otimes\pi_{j}^{*}\omega

obtained by pull-back along
\pi_{j}
(for
j=1,2
) of the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism at level
U

(i.e. the case
{\mathfrak{P}}={\mathcal{O}}_{F}
), we thus obtain morphisms

(13)

\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\pi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}

(whose tensor product is
\mathrm{Nm}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}({\mathfrak{P}})
times the isomorphism of Remark
3.2.2
).

We claim that the morphisms (
13
) extend the canonical isomorphisms

\pi_{1,K}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U,K}/K}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),K}/K}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\pi_{2,K}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U,K}/K}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),K}/K}

induced by the étale morphisms
\pi_{1,K}
and
\pi_{2,K}
(writing
\cdot_{K}
for base-changes
from
{\mathcal{O}}
to
K
).

We can again reduce to the case that
U
is sufficiently small that
h
is a closed immersion,
and replace
Y_{U,K}
replaced by
\widetilde{Y}_{U,K}
,
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),K}
by
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),K}
, etc.
Furthermore, it suffices to prove the desired equality holds on fibres at geometric closed points of
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),K}
.

To that end, we first recall the description on fibres of the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism over
\widetilde{Y}_{U,K}
.
Since we are now working in characteristic zero, the filtration on
\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U,K}/K}
obtained from
[
Dia23
, Thm. 3.3.1]

has a canonical splitting giving an isomorphism

(14)

\bigoplus_{\theta\in\Sigma}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{\theta,K}^{-1}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta,K}\right)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U,K}/K}.

It follows from its construction that it is dual to the isomorphism whose fibre at the point
y\in\widetilde{Y}_{U}(\overline{K})
,
corresponding to a tuple
\underline{A}
, is the map

d:{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(\widetilde{Y}_{U,K})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\theta\in\Sigma}{\operatorname{Hom}\,}_{\overline{K}}(H^{0}(A,\Omega^{1}_{A/\overline{K}})_{\theta},H^{1}(A,{\mathcal{O}}_{A})_{\theta})

with
\theta
-component
d_{\theta}
described as follows: Let
\widetilde{y}\in\widetilde{Y}_{U}(T)
be a lift of
y
to
T:=\overline{K}[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2})

corresponding to
data
\widetilde{\underline{A}}
lifting
\underline{A}
, and let
\alpha_{\theta}
denote the canonical isomorphism

H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widetilde{A}/T)_{\theta}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A/T)_{\theta}\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/\overline{K})_{\theta}\otimes_{\overline{K}}T.

For
e_{\theta}\in H^{0}(A,\Omega^{1}_{A/\overline{K}})_{\theta}
, let
\widetilde{e}_{\theta}
be a lift
of
e_{\theta}
to
H^{0}(\widetilde{A},\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{A}/T})_{\theta}
. Any two such lifts differ by an element of

\epsilon H^{0}(\widetilde{A},\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{A}/T})_{\theta}
, which corresponds to
H^{0}(A,\Omega^{1}_{A/\overline{K}})_{\theta}\otimes_{\overline{K}}\epsilon T
under the above isomorphism.
Therefore

\alpha_{\theta}(\widetilde{e}_{\theta})-e_{\theta}\otimes 1=f_{\theta}\otimes\epsilon

for some

f_{\theta}\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/\overline{K})_{\theta}
whose image in
H^{1}(A,{\mathcal{O}}_{A})_{\theta}
is independent of the choice
of
\widetilde{e}_{\theta}
, and this image is
d_{\theta}(\widetilde{y})(e_{\theta})
.

Similarly the filtration obtained from Theorem
3.1.1
on the conormal bundle
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{K}

of
\widetilde{h}:\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),K}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{Y}_{U,K}\times\widetilde{Y}_{U,K}

has a canonical splitting giving an isomorphism

(15)

\bigoplus_{\theta\in\Sigma}\left(\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta,K}^{-1}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta,K}\right)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{K},

which combined with (
9
) gives an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\theta\in\Sigma}\left(\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta,K}^{-1}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta,K}\right)\longrightarrow\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U,K}/K}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U,K}/K}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),K}/K}\longrightarrow 0.

It follows from the construction (and in particular the choice of sign) in the proof of Theorem
3.1.1

that the first morphism is dual to the one whose fibre at the point

z\in\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}(\overline{K})
corresponding to a tuple
(\underline{A}_{1},\underline{A}_{2},\psi)

is the map

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{\widetilde{\pi}_{1}(z)}(\widetilde{Y}_{U,K})\times{\operatorname{Tan}}_{\widetilde{\pi}_{2}(z)}(\widetilde{Y}_{U,K})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\theta\in\Sigma}{\operatorname{Hom}\,}_{\overline{K}}(H^{0}(A_{2},\Omega^{1}_{A_{2}/\overline{K}})_{\theta},H^{1}(A_{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{A_{1}})_{\theta})

with
\theta
-component described as follows: Letting
y_{1}=\widetilde{\pi}_{1}(z)
correspond to
\underline{A}_{1}
and

y_{2}=\widetilde{\pi}_{2}(z)
correspond to
\underline{A}_{2}
, the lift
(\widetilde{y}_{1},\widetilde{y}_{2})
is sent to

d_{\theta}(\widetilde{y}_{1})\circ\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}-\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}\circ d_{\theta}(\widetilde{y}_{2}),

where we continue to write
\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}:H^{0}(A_{2},\Omega^{1}_{A_{2}/\overline{K}})_{\theta}\to H^{0}(A_{1},\Omega^{1}_{A_{1}/\overline{K}})_{\theta}
and

\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}:H^{1}(A_{2},{\mathcal{O}}_{A_{2}})_{\theta}\to H^{1}(A_{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{A_{1}})_{\theta}
for the maps induced by
\psi
.
It follows that the diagram

\textstyle{{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\theta\in\Sigma}}\left(\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta,K}^{-1}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta,K}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\theta\in\Sigma}}\left((\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta,K}^{-1}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta,K})\oplus(\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta,K}^{-1}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta,K})\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U,K}/K}\bigoplus\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U,K}/K}}

commutes, where the horizontal isomorphisms are those of (
14
) and (
15
), the left vertical map
respects the decomposition and is defined by
(1\otimes\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*},-\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*\vee}\otimes 1)
in the
\theta
-component,
and the right vertical map is the inclusion in (
9
).
Taking top exterior powers in the resulting exact sequences (and tracking signs) then yields the desired compatibility,
which we can state as follows:

Proposition 3.2.3
.

The canonical isomorphism
\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U,K}/K}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),K}/K}

extends uniquely to the morphism
\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}
corresponding under the Kodaira–Spencer
isomorphisms to the morphism
\pi_{1}^{*}(\delta^{-1}\otimes\omega)\to\pi_{2}^{*}(\delta^{-1}\otimes\omega)

induced by
\psi
; similarly the canonical isomorphism
\pi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U,K}/K}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),K}/K}

extends uniquely to the morphism
\pi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}
corresponding
to the morphism
\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\to\pi_{1}^{*}\omega
induced by
\psi
.

Note that the uniqueness in the statement follows from the flatness over
{\mathcal{O}}
of the schemes
Y_{U}
and
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
(and the invertibility of their dualizing sheaves).

4.
Degeneracy fibres

4.1.
Irreducible components

We now turn our attention to Hilbert modular varieties in characteristic
p
, i.e., over the residue
field
k
of
{\mathcal{O}}
. We fix an open compact subgroup
U\subset\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}})
, as usual sufficiently
small and containing
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F,p})
, and we let

\overline{Y}=Y_{U,k}
and
\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})=Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),k}
.

Our aim is to describe the fibres of the degeneracy map
14
14

14

Analogous results hold for
\overline{\pi}_{2}=\pi_{2,k}
,
as can be shown using similar arguments or by deducing them from the results for
\overline{\pi}_{1}
using a
w_{\mathfrak{P}}
-operator.

\overline{\pi}_{1}=\pi_{1,k}:\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})\to\overline{Y}
,
or more precisely its restrictions to irreducible components of
\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
.
We accomplish this by generalizing the arguments in
[
DKS23
, §7]
, where
this was carried out under the assumption that
p
is unramified in
F
. See also

[
ERX17a
, §4]
for partial results in this direction without this assumption.

As usual, we first carry out various constructions in the context of their étale covers
by fine moduli schemes. Recall that the scheme
T:=\widetilde{Y}_{U,k}
is equipped with a
stratification defined by the
vanishing of partial Hasse invariants, constructed in the generality of Pappas–Rapoport models by
Reduzzi and Xiao in
[
RX17
, §3]
. More precisely, for

\theta\in\Sigma
, define
n_{\theta}=1
unless
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}
for some

{\mathfrak{p}}
,
i
, in which case
n_{\theta}=p
, and recall that
\sigma
denotes the “right shift”
permutation of
\Sigma
(see §
2.1
). We then have a surjective morphism

(16)

\widetilde{h}_{\theta}:{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\otimes n_{\theta}}

induced by
u
if
n_{\theta}=1
, and by the pair of surjections

(17)

{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\,\,\mathrel{{\mathop{\longleftarrow}\limits}}\,\,{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/T)_{\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}}\,\,\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\,\,{\mathcal{L}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\otimes p}

if
n_{\theta}=p
, and
for each
J\subset\Sigma
, the vanishing locus of the restriction of

\widetilde{h}_{\theta}
to
{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}
for
\theta\in J
defines a smooth subscheme of
T

of codimension
|J|
, which we denote
T_{J}
(see
[
Dia23
, §4.1]
).

Now consider
S:=\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),k}
, and let
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)
denote
the universal triple over
S
. Recall that for each
\theta\in\Sigma
, the isogeny
\psi
induces an

{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
-linear morphism
\psi_{\theta}^{*}:{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{\theta}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}
; its image is a rank one
subbundle of
{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}
if
\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}
and it is an isomorphism if
\theta\not\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}
.
Furthermore
\psi_{\theta}^{*}
restricts to a morphism
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{\theta}\to{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}
of line bundles over
S
, which
we denote
\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}
, and hence also induces one we denote
\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}:{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}
.

For
J,J^{\prime}\subset\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
, consider the closed subschemes
S_{J,J^{\prime}}
of
S
defined
15
15

15

Note the
deviation here from the notational conventions of
[
DKS23
]
and
[
DK23
]
, where these would be written as

S_{\sigma(J^{\prime}),J}
and
Z_{\sigma(J^{\prime}),J}
.
by the vanishing of
\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}
for all
\theta\in J
,
and
\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}
for all
\theta\in J^{\prime}
. Note that a closed point
y
of
S
is in
S_{J,J^{\prime}}
if and only
if
J\subset\Sigma_{y}
and
J^{\prime}\subset\Sigma_{y}^{\prime}
, where
\Sigma_{y}
and
\Sigma^{\prime}_{y}
were defined in §
2.6
.
Furthermore, in terms of the parameters and basis elements chosen in §
2.6
for stalks at
y
, we see that

\begin{array}[]{rl}\mbox{if $\theta\in\Sigma_{y}$, then}&\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}(f_{\theta}^{\prime}+L_{\theta}^{\prime})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}s_{\theta}(f_{\theta}+L_{\theta}),&\mbox{if $\theta\in\Sigma^{\prime}_{y}$,}\\
\theta(\varpi_{\mathfrak{P}})(f_{\theta}+L_{\theta}),&\mbox{if $\theta\not\in\Sigma^{\prime}_{y}$,}\end{array}\right.\\
\mbox{and if $\theta\in\Sigma_{y}^{\prime}$, then}&\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}(e_{\theta}^{\prime}-s_{\theta}^{\prime}f_{\theta}^{\prime})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-s_{\theta}^{\prime}(e_{\theta}-s_{\theta}f_{\theta}),&\mbox{if $\theta\in\Sigma_{y}$,}\\
\theta(\varpi_{\mathfrak{P}})(e_{\theta}-s_{\theta}f_{\theta}),&\mbox{if $\theta\not\in\Sigma_{y}$,}\end{array}\right.\par\end{array}

It follows that if
y\in S_{J,J^{\prime}}
, then
S_{J,J^{\prime}}
is defined in a neighborhood of
y
by the ideal generated by

\{\varpi\}\quad\cup\quad\{\,s_{\theta}\,|\,\theta\in J\,\}\quad\cup\quad\{\,s_{\theta}^{\prime}\,|\,\theta\in J^{\prime}\}.

We have the following generalization
16
16

16

Note however that the descriptions of the cases in the proof of

[
DKS23
, Cor. 4.3.2]
are incorrect: the first case there should
be
\theta\in(I_{Q}-I)\cap(J_{Q}-J)
, the second
(J_{Q}-I_{Q})\cup(J-I)
, and the third
(I_{Q}-J_{Q})\cup(I-J)
.
of
[
DKS23
, Cor. 4.3.2]
:

Proposition 4.1.1
.

The scheme
S_{J,J^{\prime}}
is a reduced local complete intersection
of dimension
d-|J\cap J^{\prime}|
, and is smooth over
k
if
J\cup J^{\prime}=\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
.

Proof.
Let
y
be a closed point of
S_{J,J^{\prime}}
. The description of the completion

{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),y}
at the end of §
2.6
gives
an isomorphism

{\mathcal{O}}_{S,y}^{\wedge}\cong k(y)[[X_{\theta},X_{\theta}^{\prime}]]_{\theta\in\Sigma}/\langle\overline{g}_{\theta}\rangle_{\theta\in\Sigma}

under which
s_{\theta}\mapsto X_{\theta}
and
s_{\theta}^{\prime}\mapsto X_{\theta}^{\prime}
, where

\overline{g}_{\theta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}X_{\theta}&\mbox{if $\theta\in\Sigma_{y}-\Sigma_{y}^{\prime}$,}\\
X^{\prime}_{\theta}&\mbox{if $\theta\in\Sigma^{\prime}_{y}-\Sigma_{y}$,}\\
X_{\theta}X^{\prime}_{\theta}&\mbox{if $\theta\in\Sigma_{y}\cap\Sigma_{y}^{\prime}$,}\\
X_{\theta}-\overline{\theta}(\varpi_{\mathfrak{P}})X_{\theta}^{\prime},&\mbox{if $\theta\not\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}$.}\end{array}\right.

It follows that
{\mathcal{O}}_{S_{J,J^{\prime}},y}^{\wedge}
is isomorphic to the quotient of this ring by the ideal
\langle X_{\theta}\rangle_{\theta\in J}+\langle X^{\prime}_{\theta}\rangle_{\theta\in J^{\prime}}
, and therefore to

\widehat{\bigotimes}_{\theta\not\in J\cap J^{\prime}}R_{\theta}
, where

R_{\theta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}k(y)[[X_{\theta},X_{\theta}^{\prime}]]/(X_{\theta}X^{\prime}_{\theta}),&\mbox{if $\theta\in(\Sigma_{y}-J)\cap(\Sigma_{y}^{\prime}-J^{\prime})$,}\\
\mbox{$k(y)[[X_{\theta}]]$ or $k(y)[[X_{\theta}^{\prime}]]$,}&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

In particular,
{\mathcal{O}}_{S_{J,J^{\prime}},y}^{\wedge}
is a reduced complete intersection of dimension
d-|J\cap J^{\prime}|
, and is regular if
J\cup J^{\prime}=\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}
.

\square

If
J^{\prime}=\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}}}-J
, then
S_{J,J^{\prime}}
is a union of irreducible components of
S
, which we
write simply as
S_{J}
; furthermore each irreducible component of
S
is a connected
component of
S_{J}
for a unique
J\subset\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
.

For each
J\subset\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
, we consider the restriction of
\widetilde{\pi}_{1,k}
to
S_{J}
.
We first note that the morphism factors through
T_{J^{\prime}}
, where
J^{\prime}=\{\,\theta\in J\,|\,\sigma^{-1}\theta\not\in J\,\}
. (Recall that
T_{J^{\prime}}
is the intersection of the vanishing loci of the partial Hasse invariants
{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\otimes n_{\theta}}
for
\theta\in J^{\prime}
.)
Indeed since
S_{J}
is reduced, it suffices to check the assertion on geometric closed points, which reduces to
the statement that if
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)
corresponds to an
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
-point of
S
such that

\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}:M^{\prime}_{\theta}\to M_{\theta}
and
\psi_{\sigma^{-1}\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}:L^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}\to L_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}

both vanish, then so does
\widetilde{h}_{\theta}:L_{\theta}\to L_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\otimes n_{\theta}}
(with the obvious notation).
If
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
and
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
for some
j>1
, then the vanishing of
\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}

means that

\psi_{\tau}^{*}(u^{-1}F_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)})=\psi_{\tau}^{*}(G_{\tau}^{\prime(j)})\subset F_{\tau}^{(j)},

and hence equality holds by comparing
dimensions. On the other hand the vanishing of
\psi_{\sigma^{-1}\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}
means that

\psi_{\tau}^{*}(F_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)})=F_{\tau}^{(j-2)}
, and it follows that
uF_{\tau}^{(j)}=F_{\tau}^{(j-2)}
, so

\widetilde{h}_{\theta}
vanishes. Similarly if
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
and
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}
, then
we find that

\psi_{\tau}^{*}(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})_{\tau})=\psi_{\tau}^{*}(G_{\tau}^{\prime(1)})=F_{\tau}^{(1)}

and
\psi_{\phi^{-1}\tau}^{*}(F_{\phi^{-1}\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})=F_{\phi^{-1}\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}
. Since

\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}\circ\psi_{\tau}^{*}=\psi_{\phi^{-1}\tau}^{*}\circ\operatorname{Ver}_{A^{\prime}}^{*}
and

\operatorname{Ver}_{A^{\prime}}^{*}(H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})_{\tau})=(F_{\phi^{-1}\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})^{(p)},

it follows that
\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}(F_{\tau}^{(1)})=u^{e_{{\mathfrak{p}}}-1}(F_{\phi^{-1}\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)})^{(p)}
,
which again means that
\widetilde{h}_{\theta}
vanishes.
Similarly one finds that the restriction of
\widetilde{\pi}_{2,k}
to
S_{J}
factors
through
T_{J^{\prime\prime}}
, where
J^{\prime\prime}=\{\,\theta\not\in J\,|\,\sigma^{-1}\theta\in J\,\}
.

Now consider the scheme

P_{J}:=\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}_{T_{J^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}),

where the product is fibred over
T_{J^{\prime}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{T_{J^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta})
denotes the projective bundle parametrizing rank
one subbundles of
{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}
over
T_{J^{\prime}}
. (We will
freely use
{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}
to
denote the pull-back of the rank two vector bundle associated to the universal object
over
T
when the base and morphism to
T
are clear from the context.) The image
of
\psi_{\theta}^{*}:{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}
is a rank one subbundle of
{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}

over
S_{J}
, so it determines a morphism
S_{J}\to\mathbb{P}_{T_{J^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta})
, and we let

\widetilde{\xi}_{J}:S_{J}\longrightarrow P_{J}

denote their product over
\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}
.

The proof of
[
ERX17a
, Prop. 4.5]
shows
that
\widetilde{\xi}_{J}
is bijective on closed points, and hence is a Frobenius factor,
in the sense that
some
power of the Frobenius endomorphism on
P_{J}
factors
through it. We will instead use relative Dieudonné theory, as in
[
DKS23
, §7.1]
,
to geometrize the pointwise construction in
[
ERX17a
]
of the inverse map, showing
in particular that a
single
power of Frobenius suffices.

Before we carry this out in the next section, let us remark that the closed subschemes
T_{J}
of
T

descend to closed subschemes of
\overline{Y}
, which we denote by
\overline{Y}_{J}
, and similarly
the subschemes
S_{J}
(or more generally
S_{J,J^{\prime}}
) descend to subschemes of
\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
,
which we denote
\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})_{J}
(or
\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})_{J,J^{\prime}}
). Furthermore since
we are now working in characteristic
p
, we may choose
U
sufficiently small that the natural action

U\cap{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{\times}
on the vector bundles
{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}
is trivial. The action of

{\mathcal{O}}_{F,(p),+}^{\times}
therefore defines descent data on the
{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}
to a vector
bundle over
\overline{Y}
, and denoting this too by
{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}
, we obtain a morphism

{\xi}_{J}:\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})_{J}\longrightarrow\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}_{\overline{Y}_{J^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}).

4.2.
Frobenius factorization

We will define a morphism
P_{J}\to S
corresponding to an isogeny from
A^{(p)}
, where
A
is the pull-back
to
P_{J}
of the universal abelian scheme over
T_{J^{\prime}}
. In order to do this, we first construct a certain
Raynaud
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})
-module scheme over
T_{J^{\prime}}
. To that end, we need to define a line bundle

{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}
over
P_{J}
for each
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
, along with morphisms

s_{\tau}:{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau}\quad\mbox{and}\quad t_{\tau}:{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}

such that
s_{\tau}t_{\tau}=0
for all
\tau
.

Suppose then that
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
, let
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}
.
and consider the exact sequence

(18)

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\tau}\longrightarrow 0

over
P_{J}
, where
{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}
and
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\tau}
are the line bundles defined by

•

{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}={\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}
and
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\tau}={\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}
if
\theta\in J
;

•

{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}=\ker(\widetilde{h}_{\theta})\mathrel{{\mathop{\leftarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{M}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\otimes p}
and

{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\tau}={\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}/{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\otimes p}

if
\sigma^{-1}\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\not\in J
, with

\widetilde{h}_{\theta}:{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\otimes p}
given by
(
17
) and the isomorphism
{\mathcal{M}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\otimes p}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}

induced by
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}:({\mathcal{G}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(e_{{\mathfrak{p}}})})^{(p)}{\rightarrow}{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}
;

•

the tautological exact sequence over
\mathbb{P}_{T_{J^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta})
if
\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}
.

Note that the first two conditions are both satisfied if
\theta\in J^{\prime}
, in which case the definitions coincide since

\ker(\widetilde{h}_{\theta})={\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}
on
T_{J^{\prime}}
.

We now show that
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}:{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}}^{(p)}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}}

and
\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}:{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}}^{(p)}
restrict to morphisms

{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}
,
and hence also induce morphisms

{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\prime\otimes p}\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\phi\circ\tau}
and
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\phi\circ\tau}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\prime\otimes p}
,

•

If
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J
, then

{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}={\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}=\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}(u^{1-e_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}})
, so

{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p})=0
and

\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau})\subset\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}})=u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}({\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})^{(p)}\subset{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\otimes p}={\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}.

•

If
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\not\in J
, then
{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau}={\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}(({\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})^{(p)})
, so
\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau})=0
and

{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p})\subset{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}}^{(p)})\subset{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}(({\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})^{(p)})={\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau}.

•

If
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\not\in J
and
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\in J
, then
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime}

for some
j\in\{2,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}\}
, which implies that
u{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(j)}={\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(j-2)}
. We deduce that

{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\subset{\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}={\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(j)}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}\subset u{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/P_{J})_{\tau}

(where the last inclusion holds since
u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}\subset u^{j-1}{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(j)}=u^{j-1}{\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}=0
),
and hence that
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p})\subset{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}^{(p)})\subset{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}(({\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})^{(p)}=0

and
\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau})\subset\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}})=u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}({\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})^{(p)}=0
.

We now define
s_{\tau}:{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau}
and
t_{\tau}:{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi\circ\tau}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\phi\circ\tau}

as the restrictions of
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}
and
\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}
, yielding Raynaud data
({\mathcal{A}}_{\tau},s_{\tau},t_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}}
, and we let
C
denote the
corresponding
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})
-module scheme over
P_{J}
. We similarly obtain Raynaud data from the morphisms

s_{\tau}^{\prime}:{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\prime\otimes p}\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\phi\circ\tau}

and
t_{\tau}:{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\phi\circ\tau}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\prime\otimes p}
induced by
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}^{*}
and
\operatorname{Ver}_{A}^{*}
, and
we let
C^{\prime}
denote the resulting
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})
-module scheme.

Recall from
[
DKS23
, §7.1.2]
that the Dieudonné crystal
{\mathbb{D}}(C)
is isomorphic to
\Pi^{*}({\mathcal{A}})
, with
\Phi=\Pi^{*}(s)

and
V=\Pi^{*}(t)
, where
{\mathcal{A}}=\oplus{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}
,
s=\oplus s_{\tau}
,
t=\oplus t_{\tau}
(the direct sums being over

\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
) and
\Pi^{*}
is the functor denoted
\Phi^{*}
in
[
BBM82
, §4.3.4]
. (The assertion of
[
DKS23
, Prop.7.1.3]

is for
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/p)
-module schemes, but the analogous one for
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})
-module schemes is an immediate consequence.)
Similarly we have that
{\mathbb{D}}(C^{\prime})
is isomorphic to
\Pi^{*}({\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
with
\Phi=\Pi^{*}(s^{\prime})
and
V=\Pi^{*}(t^{\prime})

(again letting
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}=\oplus{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\tau}
, etc.).
On the other hand, we have the canonical isomorphisms

{\mathbb{D}}(A^{(p)}[{\mathfrak{P}}])\cong\Pi^{*}{\mathbb{D}}(A[{\mathfrak{P}}])_{P_{J}}\cong\Pi^{*}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/P_{J})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})

provided by
[
BBM82
, (4.3.7.1), (3.3.7.3)]
, under which
\Phi
(resp.
V
) corresponds to
\Pi^{*}{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}

(resp.
\Pi^{*}\operatorname{Ver}_{A}
). Combined with the isomorphism

{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/P_{J})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})=\bigoplus_{{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/P_{J})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{p}})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\bigoplus_{{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}}\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/f_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbb{Z}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}},

induced by multiplication by
u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}
in the
{\mathfrak{p}}
-component, it follows that (
18
)
yields an exact sequence of Dieudonné crystals

0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{D}}(C)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{D}}(A^{(p)}[{\mathfrak{P}}])\longrightarrow{\mathbb{D}}(C^{\prime})\longrightarrow 0

over
P_{J}
. Since
P_{J}
is smooth over
k
, we may apply
[
BM90
, Thm. 4.1.1]
to conclude that
this arises from an exact sequence of
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})
-module schemes

0\longrightarrow C^{\prime}\longrightarrow A^{(p)}[{\mathfrak{P}}]\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow 0.

We now define
A^{\prime\prime}=A^{(p)}/C^{\prime}
, and we let
\alpha:A^{(p)}\to A^{\prime\prime}
denote the resulting isogeny and

s^{\prime\prime}:A^{\prime\prime}\to P_{J}
the structure morphism.

We now equip
A^{\prime\prime}
with the required auxiliary data so that the triple

(\underline{A}^{(p)},\underline{A}^{\prime\prime},\alpha)
will correspond to a morphism
P_{J}\to S
.
First note that
A^{\prime\prime}
inherits an
{\mathcal{O}}_{F}
-action from
A^{(p)}
, as well as a unique

U^{p}
-level structure with which
\alpha
is compatible.

The existence of a quasi-polarization

\lambda^{\prime\prime}
satisfying the required compatibility amounts to
\lambda^{(p)}
inducing an
isomorphism
{\mathfrak{P}}{\mathfrak{c}}{\mathfrak{d}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}A^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}(A^{\prime\prime})^{\vee}

over each connected component of
P_{J}
. The corresponding argument in
[
Dia23
, §6.1]

carries over
mutatis mutandis
(with
A
replaced by
A^{(p)}
,
A^{\prime}
by
A^{\prime\prime}
,
H
by
C^{\prime}
,
{\mathfrak{p}}
by
{\mathfrak{P}}
and

{\mathcal{I}}_{i}
by
{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}
, now running over
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
instead of

) to reduce this to the orthogonality of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}
and

u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}
under the pairing on
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{(p)}/P_{J})_{\tau}

induced by
\lambda^{(p)}
(via
[
Dia23
, (4)]
). This in turn is equivalent to the orthogonality of

{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}
and

under the pairing induced by

, which follows from
