Dia23
, Lemma 3.1.1]
via the implication that the resulting perfect pairing on

{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}}
is alternating.

We now define Pappas–Rapoport filtrations for
A^{\prime\prime}
, i.e., a chain of
{\mathcal{O}}_{P_{J}}[u]/(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}})
-modules

0=:{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(0)}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(1)}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(2)}\subset\cdots{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}:=(s^{\prime\prime}_{*}\Omega^{1}_{A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J}})_{\tau}

for each
\tau=\tau_{\mathfrak{p},i}\in\Sigma_{0}
, such that
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j)}/{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}
is an invertible
{\mathcal{O}}_{P_{J}}
-module annihilated by
u

for
j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

For each
\tau\in\Sigma_{0}
, consider the
morphism

\alpha_{\tau}^{*}:{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J})_{\tau}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{(p)}/P_{J})_{\tau}={\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/P_{J})^{(p)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}

Zariski-locally free rank two
{\mathcal{O}}_{P_{J}}[u]/(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}})
-modules. Note that if
\tau\not\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
, then

\alpha_{\tau}^{*}
is an isomorphism sending
(s^{\prime\prime}_{*}\Omega^{1}_{A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J}})_{\tau}
to
(s_{*}\Omega^{1}_{A/P_{J}})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(p)}
,
and we define
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime(j)}_{\tau}=(\alpha_{\tau}^{*})^{-1}(({\mathcal{F}}_{\phi^{-1}\tau}^{(j)})^{(p)})

for
j=1,\ldots,e_{{\mathfrak{p}}}-1
.

We may therefore assume that
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
for some
{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}
, so the image of
\alpha_{\tau}^{*}
is the
{\mathcal{O}}_{P_{J}}
-subbundle

u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{\otimes p}
of rank
2e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1
, and the kernel of
\alpha_{\tau}^{*}
is a rank one subbundle of

{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J})_{\tau}
. For each
j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1
, we will define
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j)}

as an
{\mathcal{O}}_{P_{J}}
-subbundle of
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J})_{\tau}
of rank
j
, and then verify the required inclusions on
fibres at closed points.

Suppose first that
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,j}\not\in J
. Since
u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(j-1)}=0
, it follows that

{\mathcal{F}}^{(j-1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}
is contained in
u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}
, so

({\mathcal{F}}^{(j-1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau})^{(p)}\subset{\operatorname{im}}(\alpha_{\tau}^{*})
. Therefore

(\alpha_{\tau}^{*})^{-1}({\mathcal{F}}^{(j-1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau})^{(p)}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}_{P_{J}}
-subbundle of
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J})_{\tau}
of
rank
j
, which we define to be
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime(j)}_{\tau}
.

Suppose next that
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,j}\in J
. We claim that

{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(j)}\subset{\mathcal{G}}^{(j+1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}.

Indeed if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,1}\in J
, then

{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}={\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}
and

{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(1)}\subset{\mathcal{F}}^{(j)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset{\mathcal{G}}^{(j+1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset{\mathcal{G}}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}.

On the other hand if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,1}\not\in J
, then
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,\ell}\in J^{\prime}
for some
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,j\}
, so that

{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(\ell)}={\mathcal{G}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(\ell-1)}=u^{-1}{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(\ell-2)}
,
which implies that
u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}{\mathcal{G}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}=u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}\subset u^{\ell-1}{\mathcal{F}}^{(\ell)}=u^{\ell-2}{\mathcal{F}}^{(\ell-2)}=0
, and hence

{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset{\mathcal{G}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset{\mathcal{G}}^{(\ell-1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}={\mathcal{F}}^{(\ell)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset{\mathcal{F}}^{(j)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset{\mathcal{G}}^{(j+1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset{\mathcal{G}}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\subset u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}.

Now define
{\mathcal{T}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(j+1)}
to be
{\mathcal{F}}^{(j+1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}
if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,j+1}\in J
, and
to be the preimage in
{\mathcal{G}}^{(j+1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}
of the pull-back to
P_{J}
of the tautological subbundle
of
{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,j+1}}
over
\mathbb{P}_{T_{J^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,j+1}})
if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,j+1}\not\in J

(in which case
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,j+1}\in J^{\prime\prime}
). In either case

({\mathcal{T}}^{(j+1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau})^{(p)}
is contained in the image of
\alpha_{\tau}^{*}
, so that

(\alpha_{\tau}^{*})^{-1}({\mathcal{T}}^{(j+1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau})^{(p)}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}_{P_{J}}
-subbundle of
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J})
of
rank
j+2
. Furthermore we have

\alpha_{\tau}^{*}(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J})_{\tau})={\mathcal{A}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{\otimes p}\subset({\mathcal{T}}^{(j+1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau})^{(p)},

so that
u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J})_{\tau}
is a rank two

{\mathcal{O}}_{P_{J}}
-subbundle of
(\alpha_{\tau}^{*})^{-1}({\mathcal{T}}^{(j+1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau})^{(p)}
. We conclude that

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime(j)}_{\tau}:=u(\alpha_{\tau}^{*})^{-1}({\mathcal{T}}^{(j+1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau})^{(p)}

is a rank
j
subbundle of
u{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J})_{\tau}
, and hence of
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime\prime}/P_{J})_{\tau}
.

To prove that the vector bundles
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j)}
define a Pappas–Rapoport filtration, we must show that

u{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime(j)}_{\tau}\subset{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime(j-1)}_{\tau}\subset{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime(j)}_{\tau}

for all
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{0}
and
j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and the desired compatibility with
\alpha
is
the statement that
\alpha_{\phi\circ\tau}^{*}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime(j)}_{\phi\circ\tau})\subset({\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(j)})^{(p)}
for all such

\tau
and
j
. These inclusions are immediate from the definitions if
\tau\not\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
,
so we assume
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
.

Note that since
P_{J}
is smooth, it suffices to prove
the corresponding inclusions hold on fibres at every geometric closed point
y\in P_{J}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
.
To that end, let
F_{\tau}^{(j)}
(resp.
F_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j)}
) denote the fibre at
y
of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(j)}

(resp.
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j)}
), and for each
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
and
j
such that

\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime\prime}
, let
T_{\tau}^{(j)}
denote the fibre of the
preimage in
{\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{(j)}
of the tautological line bundle in
{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}}
over
P_{J}
.

We also let
W=W(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
and consider the Dieudonné module

{\mathbb{D}}(A_{y}[{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}])=H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)_{\mathfrak{p}}=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},0}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)_{\tau}

for each
{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}
. Thus the modules
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)_{\tau}
are free of rank two over
W[u]/(E_{\tau})
,
equipped with
W[u]/(E_{\tau})
-linear maps

H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)^{\phi}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)_{\tau}\quad\mbox{and}\quad H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)_{\tau}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)^{\phi}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}

induced by
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{A}
and
\operatorname{Ver}_{A}
and satisfying
\Phi V=V\Phi=p
,
where we use
\cdot^{\phi}
to denote
\cdot\otimes_{W,\phi}W
. Consider also the Dieudonné module

{\mathbb{D}}(A^{\prime\prime}_{y}[{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}])=\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},0}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime\prime}_{y}/W)_{\tau}
, similarly equipped with
morphisms
\Phi
and
V
, as well as the injective
W[u]/(E_{\tau})
-linear maps

\widetilde{\alpha}_{\tau}^{*}:H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime\prime}_{y}/W)_{\tau}\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{\phi},

compatible with
\Phi
and
V
. Furthermore the morphisms
\Phi
,
V
and
\widetilde{\alpha}_{\tau}^{*}
are
compatible under the canonical isomorphisms
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)_{\tau}\otimes_{W}\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{y}/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})_{\tau}

with the maps induced by the corresponding isogenies on de Rham cohomology. Thus letting

\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}
denote the preimage of
F_{\tau}^{(j)}
in
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)_{\tau}
, and similarly
defining
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j)}
, we are reduced to proving that

u\widetilde{\alpha}_{\tau}^{*}(\widetilde{F}^{\prime\prime(j)}_{\tau})\subset\widetilde{\alpha}_{\tau}^{*}(\widetilde{F}^{\prime\prime(j-1)}_{\tau})\subset\widetilde{\alpha}_{\tau}^{*}(\widetilde{F}^{\prime\prime(j)}_{\tau})\subset(\widetilde{F}^{(j)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau})^{\phi}

for all
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
and
j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, or equivalently that

(19)

u\widetilde{F}^{\prime(j)}_{\tau}\subset\widetilde{E}^{(j-1)}_{\tau}\subset\widetilde{E}^{(j)}_{\tau}\subset\widetilde{F}^{(j)}_{\tau},

where
\widetilde{E}_{\tau}^{(j)}:=(\widetilde{\alpha}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{*}(\widetilde{F}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{\prime\prime(j)}))^{\phi^{-1}}
.

We use the following description of
\widetilde{E}_{\tau}^{(j)}
for
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
and

j=0,1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, where we let
\widetilde{T}_{\tau}^{(j)}
denote the preimage of

T_{\tau}^{(j)}
in
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}/W)_{\tau}
for
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime\prime}
.

•

Since
\widetilde{\alpha}_{\tau}^{*}(\widetilde{F}^{\prime\prime(0)}_{\tau})=p\widetilde{\alpha}_{\tau}^{*}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{y}^{\prime\prime}/W)_{\tau})=pu^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\widetilde{A}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{\phi}=u\widetilde{A}^{\phi}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}
, where

\widetilde{A}_{\tau}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{F}^{(1)}_{\tau},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J$};\\
u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}V^{-1}(\widetilde{F}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)})^{\phi},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\in J$};\\
\widetilde{T}^{(1)}_{\tau},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J^{\prime\prime}$};\end{array}\right.

we have

\widetilde{E}^{(0)}_{\tau}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Phi((\widetilde{F}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)})^{\phi}),&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\not\in J$};\\
u\widetilde{F}^{(1)}_{\tau},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J$};\\
u\widetilde{T}^{(1)}_{\tau},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J^{\prime\prime}$}.\end{array}\right.

•

If
1\leq j\leq e_{{\mathfrak{p}}}-1
, then it follows from the definition of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{\prime\prime(j)}
that

\widetilde{E}^{(j)}_{\tau}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-1)},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\not\in J$};\\
u\widetilde{F}^{(j+1)}_{\tau},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j+1}\in J$};\\
u\widetilde{T}^{(j+1)}_{\tau},&\mbox{f $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j+1}\in J^{\prime\prime}$}.\end{array}\right.

(Note that if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\not\in J
and
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j+1}\in J
, then
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j+1}\in J^{\prime}
,
so that
u\widetilde{F}^{(j+1)}_{\tau}=\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}
.)

•

Since
\Phi((\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})^{\phi})=\widetilde{F}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{\prime\prime(0)}
, we have

(\widetilde{E}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})=p^{-1}(V(\widetilde{E}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{(0)}))^{\phi^{-1}}
, so
the formulas in the case
j=0
imply that

\widetilde{E}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\not\in J$};\\
(V(u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\widetilde{F}^{(1)}_{\phi\circ\tau}))^{\phi^{-1}},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}\in J$};\\
(V(u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\widetilde{T}^{(1)}_{\phi\circ\tau}))^{\phi^{-1}},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}\in J^{\prime\prime}$}.\end{array}\right.

Note in particular that

u\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}\subset\widetilde{E}^{(j)}_{\tau}\subset\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}

for all
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
,
j=0,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
(the case of
j=e_{\mathfrak{p}}
for
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}

being equivalent by an application of
\Phi
to that of
j=0
for
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1}
). The inclusions
in (
19
) are then immediate from the formulas

(20)

\widetilde{E}^{(j-1)}_{\tau}=u\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}\quad\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J$,}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{E}^{(j)}_{\tau}=\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}\quad\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\not\in J$}.

We have now shown that the triple
(\underline{A}^{(p)},\underline{A}^{\prime\prime},\alpha)
defines a morphism
\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}:P_{J}\to S
.
Furthermore rewriting equations (
20
) in the form

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}&\widetilde{\alpha}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{*}(u^{-1}\widetilde{F}^{\prime\prime(j-1)}_{\phi\circ\tau})&=&(\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)})^{\phi},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J$,}\\
\mbox{and}&\widetilde{\alpha}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{*}(\widetilde{F}^{\prime\prime(j)}_{\phi\circ\tau})&=&(\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-1)})^{\phi}&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\not\in J$},\end{array}

shows that
\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}(y)\in S_{\phi(J)}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
for all
y\in P_{J}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
(where we define

\phi:\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}\to\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}
by
\phi(\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j})=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,j}
). Since
P_{J}
is reduced, it follows that
\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}
factors
through
S_{\phi(J)}
. Note furthermore that if
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)
is the universal triple over
S_{J}
, then its pull-back by
\phi\in{\operatorname{Aut}\,}(k)

is isomorphic to the universal triple over
S_{\phi(J)}
, yielding an identification of
S_{J}^{(p)}
with
S_{\phi(J)}
under which the relative Frobenius morphism

S_{J}\to S_{J}^{(p)}=S_{\phi(J)}
corresponds to the triple
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)^{(p)}
over
S_{J}
. Note also that
\phi(J)^{\prime}=\phi(J^{\prime})
and

\phi(J)^{\prime\prime}=\phi(J^{\prime\prime})
, and we may similarly identify
T_{J^{\prime}}^{(p)}
with
T_{\phi(J^{\prime})}
and
P_{J}^{(p)}
with
P_{\phi(J)}
, under which
\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(p)}

corresponds to
\widetilde{\xi}_{\phi(J)}
and
\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}^{(p)}
to
\widetilde{\zeta}_{\phi(J)}
.

Finally we verify that the composites

P_{J}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}S_{\phi(J)}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}P_{\phi(J)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad S_{J}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}P_{J}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}S_{\phi(J)}

are the Frobenius morphisms (relative to
\phi\in{\operatorname{Aut}\,}(k)
).

Indeed it suffices to check this on geometric closed points, so suppose first that

(\underline{A},(T_{\theta})_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}})
corresponds to an element
y\in P_{J}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
, where
\underline{A}
corresponds to an element
of
T_{J^{\prime}}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
and each
T_{\theta}
to a line in
P_{\theta}=u^{-1}\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}/\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}
(using the above
notation with
y
suppressed, so
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
,
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
, and
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\bullet}
is the preimage in

H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A/W)_{\tau}
of the Pappas–Rapoport filtration on
F_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}=H^{0}(A,\Omega^{1}_{A/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})_{\tau}\subset H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})_{\tau}
).
Its image
\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}(y)
then corresponds to the triple
(\underline{A}^{(p)},\underline{A}^{\prime\prime},\alpha)
produced by the construction above,
and
\widetilde{\xi}_{\phi(J)}(\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}(y))
is given by
(\underline{A}^{(p)},\alpha_{\phi(\theta)}^{*}(P_{\phi(\theta)}^{\prime\prime}))
, where

P^{\prime\prime}_{\phi(\theta)}=u^{-1}\widetilde{F}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{\prime\prime(j-1)}/\widetilde{F}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{\prime\prime(j-1)}
and

\alpha^{*}_{\phi(\theta)}:P_{\phi(\theta)}^{\prime\prime}\to P_{\theta}^{(p)}
is induced by
\widetilde{\alpha}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{*}
.
From the formulas above, we have

\widetilde{\alpha}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{*}(\widetilde{F}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{\prime\prime(j-1)})=u^{-1}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau}^{(j-1)})^{\phi}=(\widetilde{T}_{\tau}^{(j)})^{\phi}

for
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime\prime}
, where
\widetilde{T}_{\tau}^{(j)}
is the preimage of
T_{\theta}
in
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A/W)_{\tau}
, and it follows that

\alpha_{\phi(\theta)}^{*}(P_{\phi(\theta)}^{\prime\prime})=T_{\theta}^{(p)}
as required.

Suppose now that
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)
corresponds to an element
x\in S_{J}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
, so that if

\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
and
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
, then

\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau}^{*}(u^{-1}\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)})=\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}
if
\theta\in J
and

\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau}^{*}(\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)})=\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}
if
\theta\not\in J

(with the evident notation).
Its image
\widetilde{\xi}_{J}(x)
then corresponds to
(\underline{A},(T_{\theta})_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}})
where

T_{\theta}=\psi_{\theta}^{*}(P_{\theta}^{\prime})=\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau}^{*}(u^{-1}\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)})/\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-1)},

and we must show that the triple
(\underline{A}^{(p)},\underline{A}^{\prime\prime},\alpha)
obtained from it by applying
\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}
is
isomorphic to
(\underline{A}^{(p)},\underline{A}^{\prime(p)},\psi^{(p)})
. To that end note that the formulas for

\widetilde{E}_{\tau}^{(j)}
imply that
\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau}^{*}(\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)})=\widetilde{E}_{\tau}^{(j)}
for all

\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
and
j=0,1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
(using the equivalence provided by
\Phi
between the cases
of
j=e_{\mathfrak{p}}
for
\tau
and
j=0
for
\phi\circ\tau
). In particular it follows from the case of
j=0
, that

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}&(\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau}^{*})^{\phi}:&H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime}/W)_{\tau}^{\phi}&\longrightarrow&H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A/W)_{\tau}^{\phi}\\
\mbox{and}&\widetilde{\alpha}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{*}:&H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime\prime}/W)_{\phi\circ\tau}&\longrightarrow&H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A/W)_{\tau}^{\phi}\end{array}

have the same image for all
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
. Combined with the fact that
\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau}^{*}
and

\widetilde{\alpha}_{\tau}^{*}
are isomorphisms for
\tau\not\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
, we conclude that the images of

{\mathbb{D}}(A^{\prime(p)}[p^{\infty}])\longrightarrow{\mathbb{D}}(A^{(p)}[p^{\infty}])\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\mathbb{D}}(A^{\prime\prime}[p^{\infty}])\longrightarrow{\mathbb{D}}(A^{(p)}[p^{\infty}])

under the morphisms induced by
\psi^{(p)}
and
\alpha
are the same. It follows that there is an isomorphism

A^{\prime(p)}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}A^{\prime\prime}
such that the diagram

\textstyle{A^{(p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{(p)}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{A^{\prime(p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{A^{\prime\prime}}

commutes. Its compatibility with all auxiliary structures is automatic, with the exception of the Pappas–Rapoport filtrations
for
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
, where the compatibility is implied by the equality

(\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau^{\prime}}^{*}(\widetilde{F}_{\tau^{\prime}}^{\prime(j)}))^{\phi}=(\widetilde{E}_{\tau^{\prime}}^{(j)})^{\phi}=\widetilde{\alpha}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{*}(\widetilde{F}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{\prime\prime(j)})

for
\tau^{\prime}=\phi^{-1}\circ\tau
and
j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1
.

We have now proved the following generalization of
[
DKS23
, Lemma 7.1.5]
:

Lemma 4.2.1
.

There is a morphism
\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}:P_{J}\to S_{J}^{(p)}
such that the composites

\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(p)}\circ\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}:P_{J}\to P_{J}^{(p)}
and
\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}\circ\widetilde{\xi}_{J}:S_{J}\to S_{J}^{(p)}

are the Frobenius morphisms (relative to
\phi\in{\operatorname{Aut}\,}(k)
).

4.3.
First-order deformations

In this section we compute the effect of the morphisms

S_{J}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}P_{J}\longrightarrow T_{J^{\prime}}

on tangent spaces with a view to generalizing the results of

[
DKS23
, §7.1.4]
.

We let
\mathbb{F}
denote an algebraically closed field of characteristic
p
.
First recall that if
x\in T(\mathbb{F})
corresponds to the data
\underline{A}=(A,\iota,\lambda,\eta,F^{\bullet})
,
then the Kodaira–Spencer filtration on
\Omega^{1}_{T/k}
(as described in
[
Dia23
, §3.3]
) equips
the tangent space of
T
at
x
with a decomposition

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T)=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{0}}{\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T)_{\tau}

and a decreasing filtration on each component such that

\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{0}}{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j_{\tau}}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T)_{\tau})

is identified with the set of lifts
\underline{\widetilde{A}}
of
\underline{A}
to
T(\mathbb{F}[\epsilon])
with the property
that for all
\tau\in\Sigma_{0}
and
1\leq j\leq j_{\tau}
,
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}
corresponds to

F_{\tau}^{(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
under the canonical isomorphism

H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dR}}}(\widetilde{A}/\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2}))_{\tau}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widetilde{A}/\mathbb{F})_{\tau}\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/\mathbb{F})_{\tau}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon].

In particular, for each
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
and
j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, the one-dimensional space

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T)_{\tau}):={\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T)_{\tau})/{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T)_{\tau})

is identified with the set of lines in
P_{\theta}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
lifting
L_{\theta}
,
or equivalently with
{\operatorname{Hom}\,}_{\mathbb{F}}(L_{\theta},M_{\theta})
, where
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
and
M_{\theta}=P_{\theta}/L_{\theta}
.

Furthermore if
x\in T_{J^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F})
, then
\underline{\widetilde{A}}
corresponds to an element of
T_{J^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}[\epsilon])

if and only if the Pappas–Rapoport filtrations
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\bullet}
have the property that

\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}F_{\tau}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon],&\mbox{if $j=1$;}\\
u^{-1}\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-2)},&\mbox{if $j\geq 2$;}\end{array}\right.

for all
j
such that
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime}
(via the canonical isomorphism if
j=1
).
It follows that
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})
inherits a decomposition into components

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau}
equipped with filtrations such that

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T)_{\tau}),&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\not\in J^{\prime}$;}\\
0,&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime}$.}\end{array}\right.

Suppose now that
y\in P_{J}(\mathbb{F})
corresponds to the data
(\underline{A},(L_{\theta}^{\prime\prime})_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}})
,
where
\underline{A}
corresponds to a point
x\in T_{J^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F})
and each
L^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}
is a line in
P_{\theta}
.
We may then decompose

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{0}}{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau}

and filter each component so that

\bigoplus_{\tau}{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j_{\tau}}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})

is identified with the set of lifts
(\underline{\widetilde{A}},(\widetilde{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\theta})_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}})
of
(\underline{A},(L^{\prime\prime}_{\theta})_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}})

to
P_{J}(\mathbb{F}[\epsilon])
such that the following hold for all
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{0}
and
1\leq j\leq j_{\tau}
:

•

\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}
corresponds to
F_{\tau}^{(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
under the canonical isomorphism;

•

if
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime\prime}
, then
\widetilde{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}
corresponds to
L_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]

under the resulting isomorphism

\widetilde{P}_{\theta}=u^{-1}\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}/\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{j-1}\cong P_{\theta}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon].

We then have that

\dim_{\mathbb{F}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime}$;}\\
2,&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime\prime}$;}\\
1,&\mbox{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.

Furthermore the projection
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})\to{\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})
respects the decomposition and
restricts to surjections
{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})\to{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau})
inducing an
isomorphism
{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})\to{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau})
unless

\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime\prime}
, in which case we have a decomposition

(21)

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})=V_{\theta}\oplus V^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}

where
V_{\theta}
(resp.
V^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}
) is identified with the set of lines in

P_{\theta}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
lifting
L_{\theta}
(resp.
L_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}
), so that
the map to
{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau})
has kernel
V^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}
and restricts to an
isomorphism on
V_{\theta}
.

Finally suppose that
z\in S_{J}(\mathbb{F})
corresponds to the data
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)

and identify the tangent space
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})
with the set of lifts
(\underline{\widetilde{A}},\underline{\widetilde{A}}^{\prime},\widetilde{\psi})

corresponding to elements of
S_{J}(\mathbb{F}[\epsilon])
. We may then decompose

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{0}}{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau}

and filter each component so that

\bigoplus_{\tau}{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j_{\tau}}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})

is identified with the set of lifts
(\underline{\widetilde{A}},\underline{\widetilde{A}}^{\prime},\widetilde{\psi})
of
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)

to
S_{J}(\mathbb{F}[\epsilon])
such that the canonical isomorphisms identify

\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}
with
F_{\tau}^{(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
and

\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
with
F_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]

for all
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{0}
and
1\leq j\leq j_{\tau}
.
Note that if
\tau\not\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
, then the isomorphism induced by
\psi
renders the conditions for

\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}
and
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
equivalent. Suppose on the other hand that
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
.
If
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J
and
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)}
corresponds to
F_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
,
then the functoriality of the isomorphisms between crystalline and de Rham cohomology implies that

\widetilde{G}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}=u^{-1}\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
corresponds to

G_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
, and consequently that
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}
corresponds to

F_{\tau}^{(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
. It follows that in this case the
\mathbb{F}
-linear map

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})\to{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T)_{\tau})
is trivial (where
x=\widetilde{\pi}_{1}
corresponds to
\underline{A}
).
Similarly if
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\not\in J
and
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}
corresponds to
F_{\tau}^{(j-1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
,
then we find that
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
corresponds to
F_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
,
so that the map
{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})\to{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x^{\prime}}(T)_{\tau})
is trivial (where
x^{\prime}=\widetilde{\pi}_{2}(z)
corresponds to
\underline{A}^{\prime}
).
The degeneracy maps
\widetilde{\pi}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\pi}_{2}
therefore induce injective maps from
{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
to

•

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x^{\prime}}(T)_{\tau})={\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x^{\prime}}(T_{J^{\prime\prime}})_{\tau})
if
\theta\in J
;

•

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T)_{\tau})={\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau})
if
\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}
but
\theta\not\in J
;

•

either of the above if
\theta\not\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}
.

Since
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})
has dimension
d
, it follows that all these maps are isomorphisms.

We now describe the map induced by
\widetilde{\xi}_{J}:S_{J}\to P_{J}
on tangent spaces
in terms of its effect on filtrations and graded pieces.

Lemma 4.3.1
.

Let
z\in S_{J}(\mathbb{F})
be a geometric point of
S_{J}
, and let
y=\widetilde{\xi}_{J}(z)
.
Then the
\mathbb{F}
-linear homomorphism

t:{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})\to{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})

respects the decompositions and filtrations.
Furthermore if
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
and
\theta=\theta_{\mathfrak{p},i,j}
, then the following hold:

(1)

If
\theta,\sigma^{-1}\theta\not\in J
, then
t
induces an isomorphism

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau}).

(2)

If
\theta\in J
, then
t({\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau}))\subset{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})
.

(3)

If
\theta,\sigma^{-1}\theta\in J
and
j\geq 2
, then
t
induces an isomorphism

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-2}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau}).

(4)

If
\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}
, then
t
induces an injection

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})\,\hookrightarrow\,{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})

with image
V_{\theta}
(under the decomposition (
21
)), extending to an isomorphism

{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j-2}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})/{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})

if
j\geq 2
.

Proof.
Suppose that
(\underline{\widetilde{A}},\underline{\widetilde{A}}^{\prime},\widetilde{\psi})
corresponds to
an element
\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
, where
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
and
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
,
and let
(\underline{\widetilde{A}},(\widetilde{L}_{\theta^{\prime}})_{\theta^{\prime}\in J^{\prime\prime}})
correspond to
its image
\widetilde{y}\in{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})
. We then have that
\widetilde{F}_{\tau^{\prime}}^{(j^{\prime})}
(resp.
\widetilde{F}_{\tau^{\prime}}^{\prime(j^{\prime})}
)
corresponds to
F_{\tau^{\prime}}^{(j^{\prime})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
(resp.
F_{\tau^{\prime}}^{\prime(j^{\prime})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
)
under the canonical isomorphisms

\begin{array}[]{crcl}&H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widetilde{A}/\mathbb{F}[\epsilon])_{\tau^{\prime}}&\cong&H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/\mathbb{F})_{\tau^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]\\
\mbox{(resp.}&H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widetilde{A}^{\prime}/\mathbb{F}[\epsilon])_{\tau^{\prime}}&\cong&H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}/\mathbb{F})_{\tau^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon])\end{array}

for
\tau^{\prime}=\tau
and
j^{\prime}\leq j-1
, and for all
\tau^{\prime}=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\neq\tau
and
j^{\prime}\leq e_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}
.
It follows also that
\widetilde{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\theta^{\prime}}
corresponds to
L^{\prime\prime}_{\theta^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
for all

\theta^{\prime}=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\in J^{\prime\prime}
such that
\tau^{\prime}\neq\tau
, or
j^{\prime}\leq j
if
\tau^{\prime}=\tau
.
This proves that
t
respects the decompositions and filtrations, and that
\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}
, then the image of

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})\to{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})
is contained in
V_{\theta}
.
Furthermore, we have already seen that
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}
corresponds to
F_{\tau}^{(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
if
\theta\in J
,
so that 2) holds.

For the injectivity in 1) and the first part of 4), recall that
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
corresponds to

F_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
if
\theta\not\in J
, so if
\widetilde{y}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})
, then

\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
. The assertions concerning the image then follow from the
fact that each space is one-dimensional.

Again comparing dimensions, it suffices to prove injectivity in 3) and the second part of 4). Applying the
injectivity in the first part of 4), and then shifting indices to maintain the assumption that

\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
, both assertions reduce to the claim that if
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\theta\in J
and
\widetilde{y}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j+1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})
, then
\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
.

To prove the claim, let
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j+1)}
denote the image of

\widetilde{G}_{\tau}^{\prime(j+1)}=u^{-1}\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
in

\widetilde{G}_{\tau}^{(j+1)}=u^{-1}\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}
under the morphism induced by
\widetilde{\psi}
.
Note that if
\sigma\theta\in J
, then
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j+1)}=\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j+1)}
,
whereas if
\sigma\theta\not\in J
, then
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j+1)}/\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{(j)}=\widetilde{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma\theta}
. Thus in either case, the hypothesis that
\widetilde{y}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j+1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})

implies that
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j+1)}
corresponds to

F_{\tau}^{\prime\prime(j+1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
. Functoriality of the canonical isomorphism
between crystalline and de Rham cohomology then implies that their preimages under the maps induced
by
\widetilde{\psi}
and
\psi
correspond, i.e., that
\widetilde{G}_{\tau}^{\prime(j+1)}
corresponds to

G_{\tau}^{\prime(j+1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
, and hence that
\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
corresponds
to
F_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
, so
\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
.

\square

Lemma 4.3.2
.

Let
t_{\tau}:{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau}
denote the

\tau
-component of the
\mathbb{F}
-linear map
t
, with notation as in Lemma
4.3.1
. Then

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\ker(t_{\tau})&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau}),&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\in J$;}\\
0,&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\not\in J$;}\end{array}\right.\\
{\operatorname{im}}(t_{\tau})&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau},&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\not\in J$;}\\
{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau}),&\mbox{if $\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J$;}\\
\widetilde{V}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}},&\mbox{if $\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J^{\prime\prime}$,}\end{array}\right.\end{array}

where
\widetilde{V}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}}
is the preimage of

{V}_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}}\subset{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{0}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})
under t
he projection from
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau}
.

In particular
\ker(t)
has dimension
\#\{\,({\mathfrak{p}},i)\,|\,\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\in J\,\}
.

Proof.
It is immediate from part 2) of Lemma
4.3.1
that if

\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
, then
t(\widetilde{z})=0
.

Suppose on the other hand that
\widetilde{z}\in\ker(t_{\tau})
. We prove by induction on
j
that

\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
for
j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1
, as well as
j=e_{\mathfrak{p}}
if

\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\not\in J
. Indeed if
\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
and

\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\not\in J
, then since
t(\widetilde{z})\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})
,
parts 1) and 4) of Lemma
4.3.1
imply that
\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
.
On the other hand if
\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
,
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J
and

j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
then since
t(\widetilde{z})\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j+1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})
,
parts 3) and 4) of Lemma
4.3.1
imply that
\widetilde{z}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})
.

This completes the proof of the description of the kernel of
t_{\tau}
, which immediately implies the formula for
the dimension of
\ker(t)
.

The fact that the image of
t_{\tau}
is contained in the described space is also immediate from
Lemma
4.3.1
. (Note that the first two cases in the description are not exclusive, but
are consistent since
{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})=0
if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J^{\prime}
.) Equality then follows
on noting that their direct sum over all
\tau
has dimension equal to the rank of
t
.

\square

Now let
Z
denote the fibre at
x
of
\pi_{J}:S_{J}\to T_{J^{\prime}}
, defined as the restriction of
\widetilde{\pi}_{1,k}
,
and consider the tangent space
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)
, which may be identified with the kernel of the
\mathbb{F}
-linear map

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})\to{\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})
. We denote the map by
s
and write it as the composite

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}}),

where the second map is the natural projection. Since each map respects the decomposition
over
\tau\in\Sigma_{0}
, we may similarly decompose
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)=\bigoplus_{\tau}{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau}

where
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau}
is the kernel of the composite

s_{\tau}:{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau}.

We may furthermore consider the filtration on
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau}
induced by the one on
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau}
,
so that if
z
correspond to
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)
, then

\bigoplus_{\tau}{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j_{\tau}}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau})
is identified with the set of its lifts

(\underline{\widetilde{A}},\underline{\widetilde{A}}^{\prime},\widetilde{\psi})
to
S_{J}(\mathbb{F}[\epsilon])

such that
\underline{\widetilde{A}}=\underline{A}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
and

\widetilde{F}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
corresponds to
F_{\tau}^{(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]

for all
\tau\in\Sigma_{0}
and
j\leq j_{\tau}
. (Note that this condition is automatic for
\tau\not\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}
,
in which case
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau}=0
.)

Lemma 4.3.3
.

The
\mathbb{F}
-vector space
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)
has dimension
|J^{\prime}|+\delta=|J^{\prime\prime}|+\delta
, where
\delta=\#\{\,\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\,|\,\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}},\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}\in J\,\}
.
More precisely, if
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
and
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
, then

\dim_{\mathbb{F}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\mbox{if $\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J$ and either
$\sigma\theta\not\in J$ or $j=e_{\mathfrak{p}}$;}\\
0,&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Proof.
It follows from the description of the image of
t_{\tau}
in Lemma
4.3.2
that if
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
, then

s_{\tau}
is surjective unless
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,e_{\mathfrak{p}}},\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J
, in which case the image of
s_{\tau}
is

{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau})
, which has codimension one in
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau}
. Therefore the image of
s
has
codimension
\delta
in
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})
, and the kernel of
s
has dimension

\dim_{\mathbb{F}}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J}))-\dim_{\mathbb{F}}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}}))+\dim_{\mathbb{F}}({\operatorname{coker}\,}s)=|J^{\prime}|+\delta.

Since
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau})\leq\dim_{\mathbb{F}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})=1
for all
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
and

j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and
\delta
is the total number of
\tau
and
j
such that if
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J
and either

\sigma\theta\not\in J
or
j=e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, it suffices to prove that
{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau})=0
if either
\theta\not\in J
, or

j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j},\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j+1}\in J
. This is immediate from parts 1), 3) and 4) of Lemma
4.3.1
,
which show that the following maps induced by
s_{\tau}
are isomorphisms:

•

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})\longrightarrow{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau})

in the case
\sigma^{-1}\theta,\theta\not\in J
;

•

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})\longrightarrow{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J})_{\tau})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau})
in the case
\theta,\sigma\theta\in J
,
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
;

•

{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(S_{J})_{\tau})\longrightarrow V_{\theta}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{x}(T_{J^{\prime}})_{\tau})
in the case
\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}
.

4.4.
Unobstructed deformations

We maintain the notation of the preceding section, so that in particular
J
is a subset of
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}
,

J^{\prime}=\{\,\theta\in J\,|\,\sigma^{-1}\theta\not\in J\,\}
, and
Z
is the fibre of
\pi_{J}:S_{J}\to T_{J^{\prime}}

at a geometric point
x\in T_{J^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F})
. Following on from the description of the tangent space
in Lemma
4.3.3
, we continue the study of the local structure of
Z
at each closed point
z
in order to
prove a generalization of
[
DKS23
, Lemma 7.1.6]
, showing in particular that the reduced subscheme is smooth.
We remark however that if
{\mathfrak{P}}
is divisible by a prime
{\mathfrak{p}}
ramified in
F
, then the fibres over
T_{J^{\prime}}
of
\widetilde{\xi}_{J}
need not be
totally inseparable, so the desired conclusions do not immediately follow as in
[
DKS23
]
from the commutative algebra results of
[
KN82
]

and the description of the morphisms on tangent spaces. We therefore resort to a more detailed analysis of the deformation
theory.

Fix
m\geq 0
and let
R=\mathbb{F}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]
and
R_{n}=R/{\mathfrak{m}}^{n+1}
for
n\geq 0
, where

{\mathfrak{m}}={\mathfrak{m}}_{R}=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})
. Similarly let
\widetilde{R}=W_{2}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]

and
\widetilde{R}_{n}=\widetilde{R}/I^{n+1}
where
W_{2}=W_{2}(\mathbb{F})
and
I=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})
,
so that
\widetilde{R}_{n}
is flat over
W_{2}
and
\widetilde{R}_{n}/p\widetilde{R}_{n}=R_{n}
. We let

\widetilde{\phi}=\widetilde{\phi}_{n}:\widetilde{R}_{n}\to\widetilde{R}_{n}
denote the lift of the absolute
Frobenius
\phi=\phi_{n}
on
R_{n}
defined by
\widetilde{\phi}(X_{j})=X_{j}^{p}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
.
We view
\widetilde{R}_{n+1}
(as well as
\widetilde{R}_{n}
and
R_{n+1}
) as a divided power thickening of
R_{n}

with divided powers defined by

18

We could not directly define
Z(R_{n})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
as the set of
R_{n}
-points of a closed subscheme
since the construction of
\varepsilon_{\tau}(z_{n})
relied on the existence and choice of flat

-lifts. An alternative approach
would be to prove a suitable generalization of
[
DKS23
, Lemma 7.2.1]
(the “crystallization lemma”), and use it together with the flatness
of
\widetilde{\xi}_{J}
to define
\varepsilon_{\tau}
over
Z_{\operatorname{red}}
, without knowing a priori that it is smooth. We opted instead here
for a bootstrapping argument that will allow us first to establish the smoothness of
Z_{\operatorname{red}}
so that we can apply a more direct
generalization of the crystallization lemma proved in
[
DKS23
]
.

In particular, if
z_{1}\in{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)
(viewed as an element of
Z(R_{1})
, with
R_{1}=\mathbb{F}[X]/(X^{2})
), then

\varepsilon_{\tau}(z_{1})-\varepsilon_{\tau}(z)\otimes 1
induces an
\mathbb{F}
-linear map

L_{\theta,0}^{\prime}\cong L_{\theta,1}^{\prime}/XL_{\theta,1}^{\prime}\longrightarrow(P_{\sigma\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})}/L_{\sigma\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})})\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}XR_{1}.

Writing this map as
\partial_{\tau}(z_{1})\otimes X
for a unique

\partial_{\tau}(z_{1})\in{\operatorname{Hom}\,}_{\mathbb{F}}(L_{\theta,0}^{\prime},(P_{\sigma\theta,0}/L_{\sigma\theta,0})^{(p^{-1})})
,
the condition on
\varepsilon_{\tau}(z_{1})
in the definition of unobstructedness is then equivalent to the vanishing of
\partial_{\tau}(z_{1})
.

More generally, if
n=1
and
m
is arbitrary, then to give an element
z_{1}\in Z(R_{1})
over

is equivalent to giving
an
m
-tuple of tangent vectors
(z^{(1)}_{1},\ldots,z_{1}^{(m)})
in
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)
, or more functorially an element
of
{\operatorname{Hom}\,}_{\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}^{m},{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z))
, the induced morphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z,z}^{\wedge}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{Z,z}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{z}^{2}\to R_{1}
being surjective
if and only if the
m
-tuple is linearly independent. Furthermore any
\mathbb{F}
-algebra morphism
\alpha:R_{1}\to R_{1}^{\prime}
(for arbitrary
m,m^{\prime}
)
admits a lift
\widetilde{\alpha}:\widetilde{R}_{1}\to\widetilde{R}^{\prime}_{1}
, so the function

(23)

\partial_{\tau}:{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}\,}_{\mathbb{F}}(L_{\theta,0}^{\prime},(P_{\sigma\theta,0}/L_{\sigma\theta,0})^{(p^{-1})})

is
\mathbb{F}
-linear. (Let
z_{1}\in Z(R_{1})
correspond to a basis
(z^{(1)}_{1},\ldots,z_{1}^{(m)})
of
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)
, and
realize an arbitrary tangent vector
\sum a_{i}z_{1}^{(i)}
as the image of
z_{1}
under
X_{i}\mapsto a_{i}X
.)
The set of unobstructed elements in
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)
is thus the kernel of the resulting
\mathbb{F}
-linear map

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Delta}{\operatorname{Hom}\,}_{\mathbb{F}}(L_{\theta,0}^{\prime},(P_{\sigma\theta,0}/L_{\sigma\theta,0})^{(p^{-1})}),

where
\Delta=\{\,\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\,|\,\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}},\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}\in J\,\}
, and is therefore a vector
space of dimension at least

by Lemma
4.3.3
. The functoriality of
\varepsilon_{\tau}
also implies that an element

corresponding to an

is unobstructed if and only if each of the

is,
so it follows that there is a surjective
\mathbb{F}
-algebra homomorphism

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}\longrightarrow R_{1}=\mathbb{F}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{|J^{\prime}|}]]/(X_{1},\ldots,X_{|J^{\prime}|})^{2}

corresponding to an element
z_{1}\in Z(R_{1})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
.

We shall in fact need the following more precise description of the set of unobstructed tangent vectors.

Lemma 4.4.1
.

If
z\in Z(\mathbb{F})
, then
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)^{\operatorname{uno}\,}=\oplus_{\tau}{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)^{\operatorname{uno}\,}_{\tau}
,
where
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
(resp.
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau}^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
) is the set of unobstructed
elements of
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)
(resp.
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau}
). Furthermore, for each
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
,
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
, we have

\dim_{\mathbb{F}}{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)^{\operatorname{uno}\,}_{\tau})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\mbox{if $\sigma\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}$;}\\
0,&\mbox{otherwise,}\end{array}\right.

with respect to the filtration induced by the one on
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau}
, so
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)^{{\operatorname{uno}\,}})=|J^{\prime}|=|J^{\prime\prime}|
.

Proof.
The assertion concerning the decomposition follows from the vanishing of the restriction of
\partial_{\tau}
to

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau^{\prime}}
for
\tau\in\Delta
and
\tau^{\prime}\neq\tau
,
where
\partial_{\tau}
is defined in (
23
).
The assertion concerning the
filtration then follows from Lemma
4.3.3
and the injectivity of
\partial_{\tau}
on

{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}({\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau})
.
The desired vanishing and injectivity are both consequences of the claim that if
\tau\in\Delta
,
R_{1}=\mathbb{F}[X]/(X^{2})
and

z_{1}\in Z(R_{1})
is a lift
of
z
such that
F_{\tau,1}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}
corresponds to
F_{\tau,0}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}
under
the canonical isomorphism
H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{1}^{\prime}/R_{1})_{\tau}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{0}^{\prime}/R_{1})_{\tau}\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}^{\prime}/\mathbb{F})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}
,
then
F_{\tau,1}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}
corresponds to
F_{\tau,0}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}
if and only if
\partial_{\tau}(z_{1})=0
, i.e.,

L^{\prime}_{\theta,1}
corresponds to
L_{\sigma\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}
under
\varepsilon_{\tau}(z_{1})
.
Indeed if
z_{1}\in{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau^{\prime}}
for some
\tau^{\prime}\neq\tau
,
then
F_{\tau,1}^{\prime(j)}
corresponds to
F_{\tau,0}^{\prime(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}

for
j=0,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and if
z_{1}\in{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)_{\tau}
,
then
F_{\tau,1}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}
corresponds to
F_{\tau,0}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}
,
and
z_{1}
is trivial if and only if

F_{\tau,1}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}
corresponds to
F_{\tau,0}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}

The claim follows from the commutativity of the diagram:

\textstyle{P_{\theta,0}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{\tau}(z_{0})\otimes R_{1}}
\textstyle{P_{\theta,1}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{\tau}(z_{1})}
\textstyle{P_{\sigma\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}}

(where the top arrow is the canonical isomorphism), which in turn follows from that of

\textstyle{\widetilde{G}_{\tau,0}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}\otimes_{W_{2}}\widetilde{R}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau,0}^{*}\otimes\widetilde{R}_{1}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{G}_{\tau,1}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau,1}^{*}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{F}_{\tau,0}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}\otimes_{W_{2}}\widetilde{R}_{1},}

where the top arrow is induced by the morphisms

of

\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{0}^{\prime}/W_{2})_{\tau}\otimes_{W_{2}}\widetilde{R}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Suppose now that
m
is fixed (for example as
|J^{\prime}|
) and consider the thickening
R_{n+1}\to R_{n}
(with trivial divided powers), and consider the natural map
Z(R_{n+1})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}\to Z(R_{n})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
.
We will prove (Lemma
4.4.2
below) that this map is surjective, explaining why we call such deformations
unobstructed
. Before doing so, however, we illustrate the construction of the desired liftings with some examples.

Suppose first that
[F:{\mathbb{Q}}]=2
. If
p
splits in
F
, then

J^{\prime}=J^{\prime\prime}=\emptyset
and
\Delta=J
. Therefore Lemma
4.4.1
implies that all unobstructed deformations are trivial (i.e.,
Z(R_{n})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}=Z({\mathbb{F}})
), so
there is nothing to prove. The same holds if

p
is inert in
F
, and either
J=\Sigma
or
\emptyset
.
More explicitly, it is well-known that in the preceding cases,
Z
is isomorphic to
({\operatorname{Spec}\,}(\mathbb{F}[X]/(X^{p})))^{\delta}
(see for example
[
DKS23
, (43)]
), so
Z(R_{n})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
consists of a single element.

Suppose on the other hand that
p
is inert in
F
and
J=\{\tau_{0}\}
, where
{\mathfrak{P}}=p{\mathcal{O}}_{F}
and

\Sigma=\{\,\tau_{0},\tau_{1}\,\}
, so
J^{\prime}=\{\tau_{0}\}
,
J^{\prime}=\{\tau_{1}\}
and
\Delta=\emptyset
. In particular, the condition in the definition of unobstructed is vacuous, so
Z(R_{n})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}=Z(R_{n})
for all
n\geq 1
. Therefore the surjectivity of

Z(R_{n+1})^{{\operatorname{uno}\,}}\to Z(R_{n})^{{\operatorname{uno}\,}}
in this case amounts to the smoothness of
Z
, which follows for example from

[
DKS23
, Lemma 7.1.4.1]
(or alternatively, from Lemmas
4.2.1
and
4.3.3
above).
To illustrate the proof of Lemma
4.4.2
more explicitly in this case, suppose that
z_{n}\in Z(R_{n})
and let

(\underline{A}_{n},\underline{A}^{\prime}_{n},\psi_{n})
be the corresponding tuple. So in particular
\underline{A}_{n}=\underline{A}_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n}
and
\psi_{n}:A_{n}\to A^{\prime}_{n}
is an isogeny such that the morphisms

P^{\prime}_{0,n}/L^{\prime}_{0,n}\longrightarrow P_{0,n}/L_{0,n}\quad\mbox{and}\quad L_{1,n}^{\prime}\longrightarrow L_{1,n}

induced by
\psi_{n}
are trivial, where

P^{\prime}_{i,n}=H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n}^{\prime})_{\tau_{i}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad P_{i,n}=H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{n}/R_{n})=H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/{\mathbb{F}})_{\tau_{i}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}R_{n}

for
i=0,1
, and similarly

L^{\prime}_{i,n}=H^{0}(A_{n}^{\prime},\Omega^{1}_{A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n}})
and
L_{i,n}=H^{0}(A_{0},\Omega^{1}_{A_{0}/{\mathbb{F}}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}R_{n}
.
By the Grothendieck–Messing Theorem, lifts of
\underline{A}^{\prime}_{n}
to
R_{n+1}
correspond to lifts of the

L^{\prime}_{i,n}\subset P^{\prime}_{i,n}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime}_{n}/R_{n})_{\tau_{i}}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime}_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau_{i}}\otimes_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}

to
free rank one
R_{n+1}
-submodules

L^{\prime}_{i,n+1}
of
P^{\prime}_{i,n+1}:=H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau_{i}}
for
i=0,1
.
Furthermore
\psi_{n}
lifts to an isogeny

\psi_{n+1}:A_{n+1}=A_{0}\otimes R_{n+1}\to A^{\prime}_{n+1}
(necessarily unique and compatible with
the auxiliary data) if and only if the morphisms

\psi_{i,n+1}^{*}:P^{\prime}_{i,n+1}\longrightarrow P_{i,n+1}:=H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau_{i}}\cong P_{i,0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}R_{n+1}

induced by
\psi_{n}
send
L^{\prime}_{i,n+1}
to
L_{i,n+1}=L_{i,0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}R_{n+1}
for
i=0,1
. Moreover the resulting point of
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}(R_{n+1})
lies in
S_{J}(R_{n+1})
, and hence in
Z(R_{n+1})
, if and only if

\psi_{0,n+1}^{*}(P^{\prime}_{0,n+1})\subset L_{0,n+1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\psi_{1,n+1}^{*}(L^{\prime}_{1,n+1})=0.

Choosing an arbitrary lift
L^{\prime}_{0,n+1}
of
L^{\prime}_{0,n}
and setting
L^{\prime}_{1,n+1}=\ker(\psi_{1,n+1}^{*})
ensures these conditions are satisfied (see the first part of the proof of Lemma
4.4.2
for the former). Note that these imply that
\psi_{i,n+1}^{*}(L^{\prime}_{i,n+1})\subset L_{i,n+1}
for
i=0,1
, and hence supply the desired lift of
z_{n}
to an element
z_{n+1}\in Z(R_{n+1})

Continue to assume that
[F:{\mathbb{Q}}]=2
, but suppose now that
p
is ramified in
F
. Let
{\mathfrak{P}}
be the unique prime over
p
, and write
\Sigma_{0}=\{\tau\}
and
\Sigma=\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}
. If
J=\Sigma
or
\emptyset
, then we again have
J^{\prime}=J^{\prime\prime}=\emptyset
(and
\Delta=\Sigma_{0}
or
\emptyset
, according to whether
J=\Sigma
or
\emptyset
). Again it follows from Lemma
4.3.3
that all unobstructed lifts are trivial, so there is nothing to prove.

On the other hand if
J=\{\theta_{1}\}
or
\{\theta_{2}\}
, then
\Delta=\emptyset
, so again all lifts are unobstructed and the desired surjectivity amounts to the smoothness of
Z
, which can again be deduced from Lemmas
4.2.1
and
4.3.3
. To see the surjectivity more explicitly, we can proceed to construct a lift of
z_{n}\in Z(R_{n})
to
Z(R_{n+1})
as in the inert case, the task at hand now being to
lift the Pappas–Rapoport filtration:

0\subset F_{n}^{\prime(1)}\subset F_{n}^{\prime(2)}=H^{0}(A_{n}^{\prime},\Omega^{1}_{A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n}})\subset H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n})\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n})

to one in
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})
with the desired properties.
In particular, if
J=\{\theta_{1}\}
, then we require both

G^{\prime(1)}_{n+1}=uH^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})
and

F^{\prime(2)}_{n+1}
be sent to
F_{0}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}
under
the induced map

\psi_{n+1}^{*}:H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})\to H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/{\mathbb{F}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}R_{n+1}.

Note that in this case
J^{\prime\prime}=\{\theta_{2}\}
, so

F^{\prime(2)}_{n}=uH^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n})
; furthermore the
first part of the proof of Lemma
4.4.2
shows that
the image of
\psi_{n+1}^{*}
is
u^{-1}F_{0}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}
. So we obtain a lift with the desired properties by setting
F^{\prime(2)}_{n+1}=uH^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})
, within which we choose an arbitrary lift
F^{\prime(1)}_{n+1}
of
F^{\prime(1)}_{n}
.
On the other hand, if
J=\{\theta_{2}\}
, then we require

\psi_{n+1}^{*}
to send
F^{\prime(1)}_{n+1}
to
0
and

G^{\prime(2)}_{n+1}=u^{-1}F^{\prime(1)}_{n+1}
to

F^{(2)}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}R_{n+1}
.
Note that in this case

J^{\prime}=\{\theta_{2}\}
, so
F^{(2)}_{0}=uH^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/{\mathbb{F}})
,
and we obtain a lift with the desired properties by setting

F^{\prime(1)}_{n+1}=\ker(\psi_{n+1}^{*})
and defining

F^{\prime(2)}_{n+1}
by arbitrarily lifting

L_{n}^{\prime}:=F^{\prime(2)}_{n}/F^{\prime(1)}_{n}\subset P^{\prime}_{n}=G^{\prime(2)}_{n}/F^{\prime(1)}_{n}
to a line
L_{n+1}^{\prime}
in

P^{\prime}_{n+1}=G^{\prime(2)}_{n+1}/F^{\prime(1)}_{n+1}
, where

G^{\prime(2)}_{\ell}=u^{-1}F^{\prime(1)}_{\ell}
for
\ell=n,n+1
.

The notion of unobstructedness can only play a more serious role if
[F:{\mathbb{Q}}]\geq 3
. Suppose for example that
[F:{\mathbb{Q}}]=4
and
S_{p}=\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
with
e_{\mathfrak{p}}=f_{\mathfrak{p}}=2
. Write
\Sigma_{0}=\{\,\tau_{0},\tau_{1}\}
and
\Sigma=\{\,\theta_{0,1},\theta_{0,2},\theta_{1,1},\theta_{1,2}\}
,
and consider the case
J=\{\theta_{0,1},\theta_{1,1},\theta_{1,2}\}
, so that

J^{\prime}=\{\theta_{1,1}\},\quad J^{\prime\prime}=\{\theta_{0,2}\}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Delta=\{\tau_{1}\}.

Let
(\underline{A}_{n},\underline{A}_{n}^{\prime},\psi_{n})
correspond to
an element
z_{n}\in Z(R_{n})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
, and consider the
associated Pappas–Rapoport filtrations

0\subset F_{i,n}^{\prime(1)}\subset F_{i,n}^{\prime(2)}=H^{0}(A_{n}^{\prime},\Omega^{1}_{A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n}})_{\tau_{i}}\subset H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dR}}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n})_{\tau_{i}}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n})_{\tau_{i}}

for
i=0,1
. The task now is to lift these to filtrations
in
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau_{i}}
with the desired properties relative to the
R_{n+1}[u]/(u^{2})
-linear maps

\psi_{i,n+1}^{*}:H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau_{i}}\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau_{i}}\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/{\mathbb{F}})_{\tau_{i}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}R_{n+1}.

More precisely, lifting
z_{n}
to
Z(R_{n+1})
is equivalent (by the Grothendieck–Messing Theorem) to defining chains of free
R_{n+1}
-submodules

0=F_{i,n+1}^{\prime(0)}\subset F_{i,n+1}^{\prime(1)}\subset F_{i,n+1}^{\prime(2)}

of
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau_{i}}
for
i=0,1
such that

•

F_{i,n+1}^{\prime(j)}\otimes_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}=F_{i,n}^{\prime(j)}
for
i=0,1
,
j=1,2
;

•

uF_{i,n+1}^{\prime(1)}=0
and
uF_{i,n+1}^{\prime(2)}\subset F_{i,n+1}^{\prime(1)}
for
i=0,1
;

•

\psi_{n+1}^{*}(G_{i,n+1}^{\prime(j)})\subset F_{i,0}^{(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}
if
\theta_{i,j}\in J
, where

G_{i,n+1}^{\prime(j)}:=u^{-1}F_{i,n+1}^{\prime(j-1)}
;

•

\psi_{n+1}^{*}(F_{0,n+1}^{\prime(2)})\subset F_{0,0}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}
;

and we must furthermore ensure that the resulting lift

z_{n+1}\in Z(R_{n+1})
is unobstructed.

Note that since
G^{\prime(1)}_{i,n+1}=uH^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})
, the
cases with
j=1
in the third bullet amount to requiring the image of
\psi_{i,n+1}^{*}
to be
u^{-1}F_{i,0}^{(1)}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}R_{n+1}
. This is again a special case of the assertion established in the first part of the proof of Lemma
4.4.2
; we remark that for
i=0
(so
\sigma^{-1}\theta,\theta\in J
and
\phi^{-1}\circ\tau\in\Delta
, where
\theta=\theta_{0,1}
and
\tau=\tau_{0}
), the argument makes crucial use of the assumption that
z_{n}
is unobstructed. Furthermore, since
\theta_{0,2}\in J^{\prime\prime}
, we
have
F^{\prime(2)}_{0,n}=uH^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}_{n}/R_{n})_{\tau_{0}}
.
It follows that all the conditions with
i=0
are satisfied if we set
F^{\prime(2)}_{0,n+1}=uH^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime}_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau_{0}}
and choose within it an arbitrary lift
F^{\prime(1)}_{0,n+1}
of

F^{\prime(1)}_{0,n}
.

The third bullet for
i=1
,
j=2
translates into the
requirement that
G^{\prime(2)}_{1,n+1}\subset(\psi_{1,n+1}^{*})^{-1}(F^{(2)}_{1,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1})
, which forces equality since each is free of rank
3
over
R_{n+1}
. Note also that

\psi_{1,n+1}^{*}(uH^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime}_{n}/R_{n+1}))=F^{(1)}_{1,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}\subset F^{(2)}_{1,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1},

so we obtain the desired lift of
F_{1,n}^{\prime(1)}
by setting

F^{\prime(1)}_{1,n+1}=u(\psi_{1,n+1}^{*})^{-1}(F^{(2)}_{1,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1})
.
Finally we could choose
F^{\prime(2)}_{1,n+1}
so that

F^{\prime(2)}_{1,n+1}/F^{\prime(1)}_{1,n+1}
is an arbitrary lift
of the line
F^{\prime(2)}_{1,n}/F^{\prime(1)}_{1,n}\subset G^{\prime(2)}_{1,n}/F^{\prime(1)}_{1,n}
to one in

G^{\prime(2)}_{1,n+1}/F^{\prime(1)}_{1,n+1}
, thus defining a lift
of
z_{n}
to an element
z_{n+1}\in Z(R_{n+1})
. However the requirement that it be unobstructed determines a unique such line, specifically via the isomorphism (
26
) constructed in the proof of Lemma
4.4.2
.

We now return to the general setting.

Lemma 4.4.2
.

For all
n\geq 0
, the map
Z(R_{n+1})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}\to Z(R_{n})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
is surjective.

Proof.
Let
(\underline{A}_{n},\underline{A}^{\prime}_{n},\psi_{n})
correspond to an element
z_{n}\in Z(R_{n})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
.
For
\ell=n,n+1
, we let

\psi_{\tau,\ell}^{*}:H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{\ell})_{\tau}\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{\ell})_{\tau}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{0}/\mathbb{F})_{\tau}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{\ell}

denote the morphism induced by
\psi
, so that
\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}\otimes_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}
is identified with

\psi_{\tau,n}^{*}:H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n})_{\tau}\to H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{n}/R_{n})_{\tau}
under the canonical isomorphisms

H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(B/R_{n+1})\otimes_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(B/R_{n})\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(B/R_{n})

for
B=A_{n},A^{\prime}_{n}
. Similarly let
\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau,\ell}^{*}
denote the morphism

H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime}_{n}/\widetilde{R}_{\ell})_{\tau}\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/\widetilde{R}_{\ell})_{\tau}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{0}/W_{2})_{\tau}\otimes_{W_{2}}\widetilde{R}_{\ell}

for
\ell=n,n+1
, so that
\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau,n+1}\otimes_{W_{2}}\mathbb{F}=\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
and

\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau,n+1}^{*}\otimes_{\widetilde{R}_{n+1}}\widetilde{R}_{n}=\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau,n}^{*}
.

Recall that to give an element of
Z(R_{n+1})^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
lifting
z_{n}
amounts to defining suitable chains of
R_{n+1}[u]/(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}})
-submodules

(25)

0=F^{\prime(0)}_{\tau,n+1}\subset F^{\prime(1)}_{\tau,n+1}\subset\cdots F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}\subset H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}

lifting the Pappas–Rapoport filtrations on
F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n}
for all
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{0}
.
Before carrying this out, we prove that if
\tau
is such that
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J
, then the image of
\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
is

u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}F_{\tau,0}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}
.

Suppose first that
\sigma^{-1}\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i-1,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\not\in J
, so that
\theta\in J^{\prime}
and

(F_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau,0}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)})^{(p)}
has preimage
u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}F_{\tau,0}^{(1)}
under the morphism

H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/\mathbb{F})_{\tau}\to H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}^{(p)}/\mathbb{F})_{\tau}\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/\mathbb{F})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\otimes_{\mathbb{F},\phi}\mathbb{F}

induced by
\operatorname{Ver}:A_{0}^{(p)}\to A_{0}
. Consider the commutative diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}&\longrightarrow&H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime(p)}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}&\cong&H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\otimes_{R_{n},\phi}R_{n+1}\\
\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow\\
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}&\longrightarrow&H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{(p)}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}&\cong&H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{n}/R_{n})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\otimes_{R_{n},\phi}R_{n+1}\\
\parallel\wr&&\parallel\wr&&\parallel\wr\\
H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/\mathbb{F})_{\tau}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}&\longrightarrow&H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}^{(p)}/\mathbb{F})_{\tau}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}&\cong&H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/\mathbb{F})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\otimes_{\mathbb{F},\phi}R_{n+1},\end{array}

where the first horizontal maps are induced by the Verschiebung morphisms, the
horizontal isomorphisms by base-change relative to absolute Frobenius morphisms (and crystalline-de Rham isomorphisms),
top vertical maps by
\psi_{n}
(and
\psi_{n}^{(p)}
) and vertical isomorphisms by base-change
(and crystalline-de Rham isomorphisms again). The image across the top of the diagram is

F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau,n}\otimes_{R_{n},\phi}R_{n+1}
, and since
z_{n}\in S_{J}(R_{n})

and
\sigma^{-1}\theta\not\in J
, this maps to
F^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F},\phi}R_{n+1}

along the right side of the diagram. Therefore the image along the left side of the diagram must be contained
in the preimage of this, which is
u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}F_{\tau,0}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}
, and equality follows on comparing ranks.

Suppose on the other hand that
\sigma^{-1}\theta\in J
, so that
\phi^{-1}\circ\tau\in\Delta
. In this case we consider the commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime(p)}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})_{\tau}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!}
\textstyle{\!\!\!\!\!H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime}_{n}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})_{\tau}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/\widetilde{R}_{n})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\otimes_{\widetilde{R}_{n},\widetilde{\phi}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{(p)}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})_{\tau}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!}
\textstyle{\!\!\!\!\!H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})_{\tau}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/\widetilde{R}_{n})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\otimes_{\widetilde{R}_{n},\widetilde{\phi}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{(p^{-1})}/\widetilde{R}_{n})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\otimes_{\widetilde{R}_{n},\widetilde{\phi}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\!\!\!\!\!H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{0}/W_{2})_{\tau}\otimes_{W_{2}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{0}/W_{2})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\otimes_{W_{2},\widetilde{\phi}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{0}^{(p^{-1})}/W_{2})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\otimes_{W_{2},\widetilde{\phi}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

where the downwards arrows are induced by
\psi_{n}
(and its base-changes), the northeastward arrows
by Verschiebung morphisms, all the isomorphisms are crystalline transition (and base-change) maps,
and
\widetilde{\phi}
denotes both the restriction of the map
\widetilde{\phi}_{n+1}:\widetilde{R}_{n+1}\to\widetilde{R}_{n+1}

to
W_{2}
and its factorization through
\widetilde{R}_{n}\to\widetilde{R}_{n+1}
.

We have that the image of
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})_{\tau}
in
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime(p)}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})

under the morphism induced by
\operatorname{Ver}:A_{n}^{\prime(p)}\to A_{n}^{\prime}
corresponds to

\widetilde{F}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau,n}\otimes_{\widetilde{R}_{n},\widetilde{\phi}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}\subset H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/\widetilde{R}_{n})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}\otimes_{\widetilde{R}_{n},\widetilde{\phi}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}

(this being an inclusion since
\widetilde{R}_{n+1}
is flat over
W_{2}

and
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/\widetilde{R}_{n})_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}/\widetilde{F}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau,n}
is free over
\widetilde{R}_{n}/p\widetilde{R}_{n}=R_{n}
).
Since
z_{n}
is unobstructed, this submodule has the same the image along the right-hand side of the diagram as that of

u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\widetilde{F}^{(1)}_{\tau,0})^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{W_{2},\widetilde{\phi}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}

under the morphism induced by
\operatorname{Ver}:A_{0}\to A_{0}^{(p^{-1})}
. It follows that the image of
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})_{\tau}

along the sides of the top left parallelogram is the same as that of

u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\widetilde{F}^{(1)}_{\tau,0}\otimes_{W_{2}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}\cong u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\widetilde{F}^{(1)}_{\tau,n+1}

under the morphism

H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{0}/W_{2})_{\tau}\otimes_{W_{2}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})_{\tau}\to H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{(p)}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})_{\tau}

induced by Verschiebung. Furthermore the kernel of this morphism is contained in

pH^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})_{\tau}\subset u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\widetilde{F}^{(1)}_{\tau,n+1}
, so we conclude that
the image of
\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
is contained in
u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\widetilde{F}^{(1)}_{\tau,n+1}
. Therefore
the image of
\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
is contained in
u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}F^{(1)}_{\tau,n+1}
, and equality follows on comparing ranks.

We now proceed to define the lifts of the Pappas-Rapoport filtration as in (
25
). If
\tau\not\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
,
then
\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
is an isomorphism, under which we require that
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
correspond to

F^{(j)}_{\tau,n+1}=F^{(j)}_{\tau,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}
for all
j
. If
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
,
then the definition of
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
will depend on whether
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
and
\sigma\theta
are in
J

(and whether
j=e_{\mathfrak{p}}
):

•

Let
F^{\prime(0)}_{\tau,n+1}=0
and
G^{\prime(1)}_{\tau,n+1}=H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}[u]=u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}
.

•

If
\theta\not\in J
, then we let
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
denote the preimage of
F_{\tau,n+1}^{(j-1)}
under
\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
,
and if
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, then let
G^{\prime(j+1)}_{\tau,n+1}=u^{-1}F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
. Note that since

F_{\tau,n+1}^{(j-1)}\subset u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-j+1}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}\subset uH^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}\subset{\operatorname{im}}(\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*})

and
\ker(\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*})
is free of rank one over
R_{n+1}
, it follows that
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
is free of rank
j
over
R_{n+1}
.
In particular
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
is annihilated by
u^{j}
, so if
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, then
G^{\prime(j+1)}_{\tau,n+1}
is free of rank
j+2

over
R_{n+1}
. Furthermore
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}\otimes_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}
is identified with the preimage of
F_{\tau,n}^{(j-1)}

under
\psi_{\tau,n}^{*}
, which is
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n}
since
\theta\not\in J
, and hence also
G^{\prime(j+1)}_{\tau,n+1}\otimes_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}=G^{\prime(j+1)}_{\tau,n}
if
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

•

If
\theta\in J
,
\sigma\theta\in J
and
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, then let
G^{\prime(j+1)}_{\tau,n+1}
denote the preimage of

F_{\tau,n+1}^{(j+1)}
under
\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
and let
F_{\tau,n+1}^{\prime(j)}=uG_{\tau,n+1}^{\prime(j+1)}
. Note that
if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\not\in J
, then
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,\ell}\in J^{\prime}
for some
\ell
such that
2\leq\ell\leq j
, so

uF_{\tau,n+1}^{(\ell)}=F_{\tau,n+1}^{(\ell-2)}
, which implies that

u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}\subset F_{\tau,n+1}^{(j+1)}\subset uH^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}\subset{\operatorname{im}}(\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}).

On the other hand if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J
, then we have shown that

{\operatorname{im}}(\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*})=u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}F_{\tau,n+1}^{(1)}
, which again contains
F_{\tau,n+1}^{(j+1)}
, and

\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau})\subset F_{\tau,n+1}^{(1)}\subset F_{\tau,n+1}^{(j+1)}.

Therefore in either case,
G_{\tau,n+1}^{\prime(j+1)}
is free over
R_{n+1}
of rank
j+2
and contains

u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}
, so that
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
is free of rank
j

over
R_{n+1}
. Furthermore
G^{\prime(j+1)}_{\tau,n+1}\otimes_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}
is identified with the preimage of

F_{\tau,n}^{(j+1)}
under
\psi_{\tau,n}^{*}
, which is
G^{\prime(j+1)}_{\tau,n}
since
\sigma\theta\in J
, and hence
also
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}\otimes_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}=F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n}
.

•

If
\theta\in J
and
\sigma\theta\not\in J
, then we have defined
F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,n+1}

and
G^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
above, with the property that

P^{\prime}_{\theta,n+1}:=G^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}/F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,n+1}

is free of rank two over
R_{n+1}
and annihilated by
u
. Furthermore
P^{\prime}_{\theta,n+1}\otimes_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}

is identified with
P^{\prime}_{\theta,n}=G^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n}/F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,n}
, and we define

F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
so that

F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,n+1}\subset F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}\subset G^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}

and
L^{\prime}_{\theta,n+1}:=F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}/F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,n+1}

is an arbitrary lift of
L^{\prime}_{\theta,n}=F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n}/F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,n}
to a free rank
one submodule of
P^{\prime}_{\theta,n+1}
.

•

Finally if
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\in J
and
\sigma\theta\in J
(so
\tau\in\Delta
), then we have already defined

F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}_{\tau,n+1}\subset G^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}

lifting
F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}_{\tau,n}\subset G^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n}
. Furthermore we claim that

\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}(G^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1})=F^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}
. Note that it suffices to prove

\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}(G^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1})\subset F^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}
since
G^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}

is free of rank
e_{\mathfrak{p}}+1
over
R_{n+1}
and contains
\ker(\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*})
, which is free of rank one.
If
e_{\mathfrak{p}}=1
, then the desired equality is a special case of the fact that if
