\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J
, then

\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/R_{n+1})_{\tau})=u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}F^{(1)}_{\tau,n+1}
, so assume
e_{\mathfrak{p}}>1
.
If
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}\not\in J
, then
\theta\in J^{\prime}
, so
uF^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}=F^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-2)}_{\tau,n+1}
,
whose preimage under
\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
is defined to be
F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}_{\tau,n+1}
, so
\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}

maps
G^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}=u^{-1}F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}_{\tau,n+1}
to
u^{-1}F^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-2)}_{\tau,n+1}=F^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}
. Finally if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}\in J
, then we defined
G^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}

as the preimage of
F^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}
. This completes the proof of the claim, which implies that the
restriction of
\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
defines a surjection
\widetilde{G}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}\to\widetilde{F}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}=\widetilde{F}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,0}\otimes_{W_{2}}\widetilde{R}_{n+1}
,
where as usual
\widetilde{G}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}
denotes the preimage of

G^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}
in
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}^{\prime}/\widetilde{R}_{n+1})_{\tau}
. We thus obtain an
isomorphism

(26)

P^{\prime}_{\theta,n+1}:=G^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}/F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}_{\tau,n+1}\longrightarrow P^{(p^{-1})}_{\sigma\theta,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}

exactly as in the construction of
\varepsilon_{\tau}(z_{n})
, and we define

F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}
so that

F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}_{\tau,n+1}\subset F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}\subset G^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}

and
F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}/F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}_{\tau,n+1}
corresponds under (
26
) to

L_{\sigma\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}
. Note that
F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}
is
free of rank
e_{\mathfrak{p}}
over
R_{n+1}
, and lifts
F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n}
since (
26
) lifts

\varepsilon_{\tau}(z_{n})
.

We have now defined an
R_{n+1}[u]/u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-submodule

F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
of
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime}_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau}
lifting

F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n}\subset H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}_{n}/R_{n})_{\tau}\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime}_{n}/R_{n})_{\tau}

for all
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
,
j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Furthermore each
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
is free of rank
j
over
R_{n+1}
, and corresponds to
F^{(j)}_{\tau,n+1}

under
\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
if
\tau\not\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
, so to complete the proof of the lemma it suffices to show
that the following hold for all
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
:

•

The inclusions (
25
) hold with successive quotients annihilated by
u
, and hence (by the Grothendieck–Messing Theorem, as explained at the end of §
2.4
)
determine data
\underline{A}^{\prime}_{n+1}

corresponding to a point of
\widetilde{Y}(R_{n+1})
lifting
\underline{A}^{\prime}_{n}
so that (
25
) corresponds to the Pappas–Rapoport
filtration under the canonical isomorphism
H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A^{\prime}_{n+1}/R_{n+1})\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}_{n+1}/R_{n+1})
.

•

If
1\leq j\leq e_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\not\in J
(resp.
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J
), then
\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}
sends

F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
to
F^{(j-1)}_{\tau,n+1}
(resp.
G^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}=u^{-1}F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,n+1}

to
F^{(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
), so that
\psi_{n}
extends (again by the Grothendieck–Messing Theorem) to an isogeny
\psi_{n+1}
and
(\underline{A}_{n+1},\underline{A}^{\prime}_{n+1},\psi_{n+1})

corresponds to a point
z_{n+1}\in Z(R_{n+1})
lifting
z_{n}

(where
\underline{A}_{n+1}=\underline{A}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}R_{n+1}
).

•

If
\tau\in\Delta
(i.e.,
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}
are both in
J
), then

F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}/F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}_{\tau,n+1}
corresponds
to
(F^{(1)}_{\phi\circ\tau,0})^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{n+1}
under
\varepsilon_{\tau}(z_{n+1})
,
so that
z_{n+1}\in Z^{\operatorname{uno}\,}(R_{n+1})
.

We start with the second assertion, which is immediate from the definition of
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\not\in J
,
so suppose that
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J
. Recall we proved that if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}\in J
, then

\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau})=u^{1-e_{\mathfrak{p}}}F^{(1)}_{\tau,n+1}
, so it follows that

\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}(G^{\prime(1)}_{\tau,n+1})=\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*}(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})_{\tau})\subset F^{(1)}_{\tau,n+1}.

Therefore the assertion holds for
j=1
. Note also that if
j>1
and
\sigma^{-1}\theta\in J
, then the assertion is immediate
from the definition of
G^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
. On the other hand if
j>1
and
\sigma^{-1}\theta\not\in J
, then

\theta\in J^{\prime}
, so
F^{(j)}_{\tau,n+1}=u^{-1}F^{(j-2)}_{\tau,n+1}
, and therefore

\begin{array}[]{rcl}G_{\tau,n+1}^{\prime(j)}=u^{-1}F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,n+1}&=&u^{-1}(\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*})^{-1}(F^{(j-2)}_{\tau,n+1})\\
&=&(\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*})^{-1}(u^{-1}F^{(j-2)}_{\tau,n+1})=(\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*})^{-1}(F^{(j)}_{\tau,n+1}).\end{array}

For the first assertion, we need to show that if
1\leq j\leq e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, then

(27)

uF^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}\subset F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,n+1}\subset F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}.

Suppose that
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\not\in J
, so
F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}=(\psi^{*}_{\tau,n+1})^{-1}(F_{\tau,n+1}^{(j-1)})
.
In particular if
j=1
, then
F^{\prime(1)}_{\tau,n+1}=\ker(\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*})\subset G^{\prime(1)}_{\tau,n+1}
, so
we may assume
j\geq 2
. If
\sigma^{-1}\theta\not\in J
, then (
27
) is immediate from
the fact that
uF_{\tau,n+1}^{(j-1)}\subset F_{\tau,n+1}^{(j-2)}\subset F_{\tau,n+1}^{(j-1)}
, and if
\sigma^{-1}\theta\in J
,
then we have shown that
u^{-1}F_{\tau,n+1}^{\prime(j-2)}=G_{\tau,n+1}^{\prime(j-1)}=F_{\tau,n+1}^{\prime(j)}
, so
the inclusions in (
27
) are precisely the ones in the definition of
F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,n+1}
.
On the other hand if
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J
, then (
27
) is immediate from the definition of

F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}
if either
\sigma\theta\not\in J
or
j=e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, so suppose that

\theta,\sigma\theta\in J
and
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Since

G_{\tau,n+1}^{\prime(\ell)}=(\psi_{\tau,n+1}^{*})^{-1}(F^{(\ell)}_{\tau,n+1})
for
\ell=j,j+1
, we have

F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}=uG^{\prime(j+1)}_{\tau,n+1}\subset G^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,n+1}\subset G^{\prime(j+1)}_{\tau,n+1},

which in turn implies (
27
).

Finally the third assertion is immediate from the definition of
F^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau,n+1}
and the fact that (
26
)
is identified with
\varepsilon_{\tau}(z_{n+1})
via the canonical isomorphism

H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n}/R_{n+1})\cong H^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(A_{n+1}/R_{n+1})\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{n+1}/R_{n+1}).

\square

4.5.
Local structure

We now use the results of the previous sections to prove a generalization of
[
DKS23
, Lemma 7.1.6]
, describing
the local structure of the fibres of the projection
\pi_{J}:S_{J}\to T_{J^{\prime}}
at geometric points. As before, we let
z\in S_{J}(\mathbb{F})
,

x=\pi_{J}(z)\in T_{J^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F})
, and
Z
denote the (geometric) fibre of
\pi_{J}
at
x
.

Lemma 4.5.1
.

There are isomorphisms

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}\cong\mathbb{F}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta},X_{1},...,X_{m}]]/(T_{1}^{p},\ldots,T_{\delta}^{p})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z_{{\operatorname{red}}},z}\cong\mathbb{F}[[X_{1},...,X_{m}]],

where
m=|J^{\prime}|=|J^{\prime\prime}|
and
\delta=|\Delta|
.
In particular,
Z_{\operatorname{red}}
is smooth of dimension
m
over
\mathbb{F}
. Moreover the closed immersion
Z_{\operatorname{red}}\hookrightarrow Z

identifies
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z_{\operatorname{red}})
with
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
.

Proof.
First consider the morphism
\chi_{J}=\widetilde{\zeta}_{J}^{(p^{-1})}:P_{J}^{(p^{-1})}\to S_{J}
, where
\zeta_{J}
is the morphism
of Lemma
4.2.1
; thus
\chi_{J}
is finite, flat, and bijective on closed points. Let
W
denote
the fibre of
\pi_{J}\circ\chi_{J}
at
x
, so that the fibre of
\chi_{J}
at
x
is a finite, flat morphism
W\to Z
which is
bijective on closed points, and let
w=\widetilde{\xi}_{J}(z)^{(p^{-1})}
denote the unique element of
W(\mathbb{F})
mapping to
z
.
Since the completion of
W
at
w
is the same as its completion at the fibre of
\chi_{J}
at
z
, the resulting morphism

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}\longrightarrow\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{W,w}

is finite, flat, and in particular injective. Furthermore choosing parameters on
P_{J,\mathbb{F}}
at
y:=\widetilde{\xi}_{J}(z)=w^{(p)}

so that the morphism on completions induced by the projection
P_{J}\to T_{J^{\prime}}
takes the form

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{T_{J^{\prime},\mathbb{F}},x}\cong\mathbb{F}[[Y_{m+1},\ldots,Y_{d}]]\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}[[Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{d}]]\cong\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{P_{J,\mathbb{F}},y},

we see that
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{W,w}\cong\mathbb{F}[[Y_{1}^{1/p},\ldots,Y_{d}^{1/p}]]/(Y_{m+1},\ldots,Y_{d})
.
Note in particular that
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{{\operatorname{red}}}_{W,w}
is a domain of dimension
m
and that if
r
is in the nilradical of

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{W,w}
, then
r^{p}=0
. It follows that the same assertions hold with

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{W,w}
replaced by
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}
.

Now we apply the results of §
4.4
to obtain a surjective
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
morphism

(28)

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}\longrightarrow R=\mathbb{F}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]].

More precisely, by Lemma
4.4.2
there exists an element of

\varprojlim Z^{{\operatorname{uno}\,}}(R_{n})\subset\varprojlim Z(R_{n})=Z(R)

lifting the morphism (
24
), whose surjectivity implies that of the morphism (
28
)
induced by
{\operatorname{Spec}\,}(R)\to Z
. Since the morphism (
28
) factors through
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}^{\operatorname{red}}
,
which is a domain of dimension
m
, the resulting morphism
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}^{\operatorname{red}}\to R
must be injective, and
hence an isomorphism. Furthermore since
Z
is Nagata, it follows that

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}},z}=\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}^{\operatorname{red}}\cong\mathbb{F}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]

is formally smooth of dimension
m
, and hence
Z^{\operatorname{red}}
is smooth of dimension
m
.
Note also that the image of
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z_{\operatorname{red}})
in
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)
is the same as that of the tangent space of
{\operatorname{Spec}\,}(R)

at its closed point under the morphism induced by (
28
), which is
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)^{\operatorname{uno}\,}
by construction
of (
24
).

Turning to the task of describing
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}
, let
{\mathfrak{m}}
denote its maximal ideal and
{\mathfrak{n}}
its nilradical,
and consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{n}}/({\mathfrak{n}}\cap{\mathfrak{m}}^{2})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{m}}/({\mathfrak{n}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{2})\longrightarrow 0.

Recall that
{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
has dimension
m+\delta
(over
\mathbb{F}
) by Lemma
4.3.3
, and we have just seen that

{\mathfrak{m}}/({\mathfrak{n}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{2})
has dimension
m
, so
{\mathfrak{n}}/({\mathfrak{n}}\cap{\mathfrak{m}}^{2})
has dimension
\delta
. We may therefore choose
elements
r_{1},\ldots,r_{\delta}\in{\mathfrak{n}}
and
s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in{\mathfrak{m}}
so that
(r_{1},\ldots,r_{\delta},s_{1},\ldots,s_{m})

lifts a basis of
{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
, and consider the surjective
\mathbb{F}
-algebra morphism

\mu:\mathbb{F}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta},X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}

sending
T_{i}
to
r_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,\delta
and
X_{i}
to
s_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,m
. Note that the kernel of
the composite
\mathbb{F}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta},X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]\mathrel{{\mathop{\twoheadrightarrow}\limits}}\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}^{\operatorname{red}}
contains
I:=(T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta})
, so must in fact equal
I
,
and therefore
\ker(\mu)\subset I
. On the other hand, from the first paragraph of the proof, we have
r_{i}^{p}=0

for
i=1,\ldots,\delta
, so
I^{(p)}:=(T_{1}^{p},\ldots,T_{\delta}^{p})\subset\ker(\mu)
. We have now shown that

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}\cong\mathbb{F}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta},X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]/J

for some ideal
J
of
\mathbb{F}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta},X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]
such that
I^{(p)}\subset J\subset I
.

It remains to prove that
J=I^{(p)}
. To that end consider the morphism
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{P_{J,\mathbb{F}},y}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{S_{J,\mathbb{F}},z}
induced by
\xi_{J}
. As it is a morphism of regular local
\mathbb{F}
-algebras
of the same dimension, namely
d
, and its image contains that of the Frobenius endomorphism, it is (by
[
KN82
, Cor. 2]
) finite flat of degree

p^{n}
where
n
is the dimension of the kernel of the induced map on tangent spaces, which by Lemma
4.3.2

is
\#\{\,({\mathfrak{p}},i)\,|\,\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\in J\,\}
. It follows that the induced morphism

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Y,y}\longrightarrow\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}

is also finite flat of degree
p^{n}
, where
Y\cong(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{F}})^{m}
is the fibre of
P_{J}
over
x
.
Similarly the composite

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Y,y}\longrightarrow\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}\longrightarrow\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}},z}

is also a morphism of regular local
\mathbb{F}
-algebras of the same dimension (now
m
) whose image contains
that of the Frobenius endomorphism, so it is finite flat of degree
p^{n^{\prime}}
where
n^{\prime}
is the dimension of the
kernel of the composite

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z_{\operatorname{red}})\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)\to{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(Y)\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{Tan}}_{y}(P_{J}).

Since the first inclusion identifies
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z_{\operatorname{red}})
with
{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)^{\operatorname{uno}\,}\subset{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z)
, it follows
from Lemmas
4.3.2
and
4.4.1
that
n^{\prime}=n-\delta
. Now consider the commutative
diagram:

\textstyle{\mathbb{F}[[Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}]]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Y,y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta},X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]/J\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta},X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]/I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}},z},}

where
Q=\mathbb{F}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta},X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]/I^{(p)}
and the dashed arrow is any
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
morphism lifting
\mathbb{F}[[Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}]]\to\mathbb{F}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta},X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]/J
.
Note that
{\operatorname{gr}\,}_{I}Q
naturally has the structure of a finite
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}},z}\cong Q/IQ
-algebra, compatible
with its structure as an
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Y,y}
-algebra. Furthermore
{\operatorname{gr}\,}_{I}Q
is free of rank
p^{\delta}
over

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}},z}
, and hence free of rank
p^{n^{\prime}}p^{\delta}=p^{n}
over
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Y,y}
;
therefore
Q
is also free of rank
p^{n}
over
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Y,y}
. Since the rank is the same as that of
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}
,
it follows that the surjection
Q\to\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}
is an isomorphism.

\square

Remark 4.5.2
.

It would in fact suffice for our purposes to know that
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Z,z}
is isomorphic to

\mathbb{F}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{\delta},X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]/J
for some
J
such that
I^{(p)}\subset J\subset I
, and the
equality
J=I^{(p)}
would follow from later considerations, but we find it interesting and satisfying to recognize it also
as a consequence of the tangent space calculations via the commutative algebra argument in the proof of
the lemma.

4.6.
Reduced fibres

In this section, we give a complete description of
Z_{\operatorname{red}}
, the reduced subscheme of the fibre
Z
of

\pi_{J}:S_{J}\to T_{J^{\prime}}
at any geometric point
x
of
T_{J^{\prime}}
. This generalizes Theorem 7.2.2 of
[
DKS23
]
, where
it is assumed
p
is unramified in
F
. With the ingredients put in place in the preceding sections, we
may now proceed by an argument similar to the one in
[
DKS23
, §7.2]
.

Recall from §
4.2
that we have a morphism

\widetilde{\xi}_{J}:S_{J}\longrightarrow P_{J}=\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}_{T_{J^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{P}}_{\theta})

which is bijective on closed points, where the product is a fibre product over
T_{J^{\prime}}
.
Taking fibres over
x\in T_{J^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F})
therefore yields such a morphism

(29)

Z_{\operatorname{red}}\longrightarrow\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0}),

where the product is over
\mathbb{F}
,
P_{\theta,0}=G_{\tau,0}^{(j)}/F_{\tau,0}^{(j-1)}=u^{-1}F_{\tau,0}^{(j-1)}/F_{\tau,0}^{(j-1)}
,

\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
,
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
and
F_{0}^{\bullet}
is
the Pappas–Rapoport filtration in the data associated to
x
.
We saw however (in the proof of Lemma
4.5.1
, where the target is denoted

Y
) that (
29
) may not be an isomorphism, but is in fact finite flat
of degree
p^{n^{\prime}}
where
n^{\prime}=\#\{\,({\mathfrak{p}},i)\,|\,\theta_{p,i,1}\in J^{\prime\prime}\,\}
.
We will use the approach in
[
DKS23
, §7.2.2]
to remove a factor of Frobenius from the relevant
projections
Z_{\operatorname{red}}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We will however need the following slight generalization of the
crystallization lemma stated in
[
DKS23
, §7.2.1]
, now allowing
p
to be ramified
in
F
and requiring only an inclusion relation (rather than equality) between images
of components of Dieudonné modules. We omit the proof, which is essentially the
same as that of
[
DKS23
, Lemma 7.2.1]
.

Lemma 4.6.1
.

Suppose that
S
is a smooth scheme over an algebraically closed field
\mathbb{F}
of characteristic
p
,

A
,
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
are abelian schemes over
S

with
O_{F}
-action, and
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},0}
. Let
\alpha_{i}:A\to B_{i}
for
i=1,2
be
O_{F}
-linear isogenies such that

•

\ker(\alpha_{i})\cap A[p^{\infty}]\subset A[p]
for
i=1,2
, and

•

\widetilde{\alpha}_{1,s}^{*}{\mathbb{D}}(B_{1,s}[p^{\infty}])_{\tau}\subset\widetilde{\alpha}_{2,s}^{*}{\mathbb{D}}(B_{2,s}[p^{\infty}])_{\tau}
for all
s\in S(\mathbb{F})

Then there is a unique morphism
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\operatorname{dR}}}(B_{1}/S)_{\tau}\to{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\operatorname{dR}}}(B_{2}/S)_{\tau}

of
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}[u]/(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}})
-modules whose fibres are compatible with the injective maps

{\mathbb{D}}(B_{1,s}[p^{\infty}])_{\tau}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{D}}(B_{2,s}[p^{\infty}])_{\tau}

induced by
(\widetilde{\alpha}_{2,s}^{*})^{-1}\widetilde{\alpha}_{1,s}^{*}

for all
s\in S(\mathbb{F})
. Furthermore if
j:{\operatorname{Spec}\,}(R_{1})=S_{1}\to S
denotes the first infinitesimal neighborhood of
s
,
then the morphism is also compatible with the isomorphisms

H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(B_{i,s}/\mathbb{F})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(j^{*}B_{i}/R_{1})

induced by their canonical isomorphisms with
H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(B_{i,s}/R_{1})
for
i=1,2
.

Now let
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)
denote the restriction to
Z_{\operatorname{red}}
of the universal triple, and
suppose that
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
is such that
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\in J
. Then for any closed point

z
of
Z_{\operatorname{red}}
, we have

(30)

\operatorname{Ver}^{*}(H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}^{(p^{-1})}/\mathbb{F})_{\tau})=F_{\tau,0}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}=\psi_{\tau,z}^{*}(G_{\tau,z}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})\subset\psi_{\tau,z}^{*}(H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}_{z}/\mathbb{F})_{\tau}),

where as usual we write
\underline{A}_{0}
for the fibre of
\underline{A}
at
z
(or
x
) and
F^{(j)}_{\tau,0}
for
(the sections of)
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau,0}^{\bullet}
, and similarly
\underline{A}^{\prime}_{z}
for the fibre of
\underline{A}^{\prime}
,

F^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,z}
for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,z}
and
G^{\prime(j)}_{\tau,z}=u^{-1}F^{\prime(j-1)}_{\tau,z}
.
We may therefore apply Lemma
4.6.1
with
S=Z_{\operatorname{red}}
,
B_{1}=A_{0}^{(p^{-1})}\times_{\mathbb{F}}Z_{\operatorname{red}}
,

B_{2}=A^{\prime}
,
\alpha_{2}=\psi
and
\alpha_{1}=\operatorname{Ver}\times_{\mathbb{F}}Z_{\operatorname{red}}
(where
\operatorname{Ver}:A_{0}\to A_{0}^{(p^{-1})}
)
to obtain an
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}[u]/u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-linear morphism

\beta:(H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/\mathbb{F})_{\phi\circ\tau})^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}={\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(B_{1}/Z_{\operatorname{red}})_{\tau}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}/Z_{\operatorname{red}})_{\tau}

which at closed points is compatible in the evident sense with the injective morphisms induced by
\psi
and
\operatorname{Ver}

on Dieudonné modules and with the isomorphisms over first-order thickenings provided by crystalline-de Rham comparisons
(cf. diagrams (39) and (40) of
[
DKS23
]
). In particular the fibre
\beta_{z}
at each closed point
z
is given by the reduction mod
p
of the unique injective homomorphism

\widetilde{\beta}_{z}
of free rank two
W({\mathbb{F}})[u]/(E_{\tau})
-modules making the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}}(A_{0}[p^{\infty}])_{\phi\circ\tau}^{\phi^{-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\beta}_{z}}
\scriptstyle{V}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}}(A^{\prime}_{z}[p^{\infty}])_{\tau}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau,z}^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}}(A_{0}[p^{\infty}])_{\tau}}

commute. It follows from (
30
) that the image
of
\widetilde{\beta}_{z}
is the preimage

\widetilde{G}_{\tau,z}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}
of
{G}_{\tau,z}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}
under reduction mod
p
, and therefore that
of
\beta_{z}
is
{G}_{\tau,z}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}
. Since this holds for each closed pont
z
, the image of
\beta
is

{\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}
, and hence it induces an isomorphism

(31)

\textstyle{(H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/\mathbb{F})_{\phi\circ\tau}/uH^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/\mathbb{F})_{\phi\circ\tau})^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}/{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}=:{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}^{\prime}.}
\textstyle{P_{\sigma\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}}

We can thus define the morphism

\mu_{\sigma\theta}:Z_{\operatorname{red}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(P_{\sigma\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})})

such that the tautological line bundle pulls back to the subbundle corresponding to
{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
under (
31
).
The commutativity of the diagram

\textstyle{(\widetilde{G}^{\prime(1)}_{\phi\circ\tau,z})^{\phi^{-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\widetilde{\psi}^{*}_{\phi\circ\tau},z)^{\phi^{-1}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}}(A_{z}^{\prime}[p^{\infty}])_{\phi\circ\tau}^{\phi^{-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{u^{e_{{\mathfrak{p}}}-1}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{V}
\textstyle{\widetilde{F}^{\prime(e_{{\mathfrak{p}}})}_{\tau,z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{G}^{\prime(e_{{\mathfrak{p}}})}_{\tau,z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\psi}_{\tau,z}^{*}}
\textstyle{(\widetilde{G}^{(1)}_{\phi\circ\tau,0})^{\phi^{-1}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}}(A_{0}[p^{\infty}])_{\phi\circ\tau}^{\phi^{-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{V}
\textstyle{\widetilde{F}^{(e_{{\mathfrak{p}}})}_{\tau,0}}

implies that on the fibre at
z
, the line
L_{\theta,z}^{\prime}
in
P_{\theta,z}^{\prime}
corresponds under (
31
) to the image of the morphism

(\psi^{*}_{\sigma\theta,z})^{(p^{-1})}:P_{\sigma\theta,z}^{\prime(p^{-1})}\longrightarrow P_{\sigma\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})}.

(cf. the proof of
[
DKS23
, Thm. 7.2.2.1]
).
If
\sigma\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}\in J
, then this image is

L_{\sigma\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})}
for all
z
, so

\mu_{\sigma\theta}
is a constant morphism.
On the other hand, if
\sigma\theta\not\in J
, so
\sigma\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}
, then
the composite of
\mu_{\sigma\theta}
with the Frobenius morphism

\mathbb{P}(P_{\sigma\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})})\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(P_{\sigma\theta,0})

is precisely the factor indexed by
\sigma\theta
in (
29
).

Now for any
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in\Sigma
, let

P_{\theta,0}^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}P^{(p^{-1})}_{\theta,0},&\mbox{if $j=1$;}\\
P_{\theta,0},&\mbox{if $j>1$.}\end{array}\right.

Replacing the
\theta
-factor in (
29
) with
\mu_{\theta}
for each

\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J^{\prime\prime}
such that
j=1
therefore yields a commutative diagram

\textstyle{Z_{\operatorname{red}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{eqn:naive})}
\textstyle{{\displaystyle\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}(P^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})}_{\theta,0})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\displaystyle\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0})}}

where the right diagonal map is the Frobenius morphism (resp. identity) on the factors indexed by

\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
such that
j=1
(resp.
j>1
). Since the schemes are all smooth over
\mathbb{F}

and the diagonal morphisms are finite flat of the same degree (namely
p^{n^{\prime}}
), it follows that the
horizontal map is an isomorphism.

In order to obtain the above isomorphism, we factored out a power of Frobenius in each component of the form
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1}
by describing the corresponding morphism to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
in terms of the data associated to
A^{\prime}
(i.e., pull-backs via
\pi_{2}
of vector bundles on
\widetilde{Y}_{U,{\mathbb{F}}}
). We can similarly describe the other components of the isomorphism in terms of the bundles associated to
A^{\prime}
, again with a shift in the index, but the relation is easier to obtain since no Frobenius factor intervenes. To that end, suppose that
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}

and
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J
for some
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and consider the
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}[u]/(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}})
-linear
morphism

\xi_{\tau}^{*}:{\mathfrak{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}}H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dR}}}(A/Z_{\operatorname{red}})_{\tau}={\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(({\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}A)/Z_{\operatorname{red}})_{\tau}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}/Z_{\operatorname{red}})_{\tau}

induced by the unique isogeny
\xi:A^{\prime}\to{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}A
such that
\xi\circ\psi
is the
canonical isogeny
A\to{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}A
. Since the image of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}

under
\psi_{\tau}^{*}
is
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(j)}
, it follows that the image of

{\mathfrak{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(j)}
under
\xi_{\tau}^{*}
is
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)}
.
We thus obtain an
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}
-linear isomorphism

(32)

P_{\sigma\theta,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}={\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma\theta}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathfrak{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma\theta}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{\theta},

under which
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{\theta}
corresponds to the image of the morphism
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{\sigma\theta}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma\theta}

induced by
\psi
. Recall that if
\sigma\theta\not\in J
, i.e.,
\sigma\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}
, then this is precisely the subbundle of

{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma\theta}=P_{\sigma,\theta}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}
defining the factor indexed
by
\sigma\theta
in the morphism (
29
). On the other hand if
\theta,\sigma\theta\in J
,
then this subbundle is
L_{\sigma\theta,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}
.

Theorem 4.6.2
.

For
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in\Sigma
such that
\sigma^{-1}\theta\in J
, let

\delta_{\theta}:P^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})}_{\theta,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}

denote the isomorphism defined by (
31
) or (
32
), according to whether
j=1
or
j>1
(and applied with
\sigma^{-1}\theta

in place of
\theta
), and let

\mu_{\theta}:Z_{\operatorname{red}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0}^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})})

denote the morphism such that the tautological line bundle pulls back to the subbundle corresponding to
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}

under
\delta_{\theta}
. Then

(1)

the resulting morphism

Z_{\operatorname{red}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0}^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})})

is an isomorphism,

(2)

and if
\sigma^{-1}\theta,\theta\in J
, then the morphism
\mu_{\theta}
is the projection to the point corresponding to

L_{\theta,0}^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})}
.

Remark 4.6.3
.

The theorem above is asserted in
[
ERX17a
, §4.9(1)]
for fibres of
\widetilde{\xi}_{J}
at geometric
generic
points of
T_{J^{\prime}}
. There is however a serious error in their argument (as already noted in
[
DKS23
, §7]
), specifically in the unjustified claim in the paragraph after
[
ERX17a
, (4.9.3)]
that one
can rearrange the choices of local parameters…

Theorem
4.6.2
thus fills the resulting gap in the proof of Proposition 3.19 of
[
ERX17a
]
.
We note also that the latter is a cohomological vanishing result,
a stronger version of which follows from the results in this paper (see Remark
5.3.8
), and its application in
[
ERX17a
]
(as well as
[
DW20
]
) is to the construction of Hecke operators, which is ultimately achieved in greater generality in §
5.4
of this paper.

4.7.
Thickening

We will now give a complete description of
Z
, generalizing
[
DKS23
, Thm. 7.2.4]
. Our approach is based on
that of
[
DKS23
]
: we will use the isomorphism of (
31
), for
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\in J
such
that
\sigma\theta\in J
, in order to extend the isomorphism Theorem
4.6.2
(
1
) to one between
Z
and a suitable thickening of the target.

To that end, let
T
denote the fibre product

\begin{array}[]{ccc}T&\longrightarrow&\displaystyle{\prod_{\sigma^{-1}\theta,\theta\in J}\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0}^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})})})\\
\downarrow&&\downarrow\\
{\operatorname{Spec}\,}\mathbb{F}&\longrightarrow&\displaystyle{\prod_{\sigma^{-1}\theta,\theta\in J}\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0}),}\end{array}

where the bottom arrow is defined by
L_{\theta,0}
and the right vertical arrow is defined by the Frobenius morphism

M_{\theta}\mapsto M_{\theta}^{(p)}
on the factors indexed by
\theta
such that
n_{\theta}=p
.
(Recall that these are the
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
such that
j=1
, and note that we could have omitted
the other factors from the definition, but we maintain them for the sake of uniformity in later formulas.)

We then let
\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}=Z_{\operatorname{red}}\times_{\mathbb{F}}T
, and let
i:Z_{\operatorname{red}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}

denote the resulting divided power thickening, i.e.,, the unique section of the projection

q:\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}\to Z_{\operatorname{red}}
. We thus have canonical isomorphisms

{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}}[u]/(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}})
-linear
morphisms

(R^{1}s^{\prime}_{{\operatorname{crys}},*}{\mathcal{O}}_{A^{\prime},\mathrm{crys}})_{\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}},\tau}\cong q^{*}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}/Z_{\operatorname{red}})_{\tau}

for all
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{0}
(where we continue to let
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)

denote the universal triple over
Z_{\operatorname{red}}
, and
s^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\to Z_{\operatorname{red}}
is the structure morphism).
Furthermore recall that to give a lift of the closed immersion
Z_{\operatorname{red}}\hookrightarrow Z
to a morphism

\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}\to Z
is equivalent to giving a lift of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
(for each
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
,

j=1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}
) to an
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}}[u]/(u^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}})
-submodule
\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}

of
q^{*}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}/Z_{\operatorname{red}})_{\tau}
such that

•

\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)}\subset\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
and

\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}/\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)}
is a line bundle on
Z_{\operatorname{red}}

annihilated by
u
;

•

the morphism

q^{*}\psi_{\tau}^{*}:q^{*}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}/Z_{\operatorname{red}})_{\tau}\longrightarrow q^{*}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/Z_{\operatorname{red}})=H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A_{0}/\mathbb{F})_{\tau}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}}

sends
\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
to
F_{\tau,0}^{(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}}

if
\tau\not\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
, sends
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}:=u^{-1}\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)}

to
F_{\tau,0}^{(j)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}}
if
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\in J
, and sends
\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}

to
F_{\tau,0}^{(j-1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}}
otherwise.

We construct such a lift by setting
\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}=q^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
unless

j=e_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\sigma^{-1}\theta,\theta\in J
, where
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}\in J
, in which case we define

\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}
so that

\widetilde{{\mathcal{L}}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}:=\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}/\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}\subset\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}/\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}=q^{*}{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}

corresponds to the pull-back of the tautological line bundle on

\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})})
under
q^{*}\delta_{\theta}
. Note that
i^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{L}}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}={\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}

since each corresponds to
L_{\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\operatorname{red}}}
under
\delta_{\theta}
.
The fact that the resulting sheaves
\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
, including those with
j=e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, satisfy the required properties
is immediate from their definition and the corresponding properties of the
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}
. The Grothendieck–Messing Theorem thus yields a triple

(\underline{\widetilde{A}},\underline{\widetilde{A}}^{\prime},\widetilde{\psi})
over
\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}

such that
i^{*}\widetilde{A}^{\prime}=A^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\prime\bullet}
corresponds to the Pappas–Rapoport filtration
under the canonical isomorphism

{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widetilde{A}^{\prime}/\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}})\cong(R^{1}s^{\prime}_{{\operatorname{crys}},*}{\mathcal{O}}_{A^{\prime},\mathrm{crys}})_{\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}}\cong q^{*}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}/Z_{\operatorname{red}}).

We claim that the resulting morphism

\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}\longrightarrow Z

is an isomorphism. We first note that the induced morphism is injective on
tangent spaces at all closed points
\widetilde{z}=(z,t)
in

\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}(\mathbb{F})=Z(\mathbb{F})\times T(\mathbb{F})\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}Z(\mathbb{F})
. Indeed let
R_{1}=\mathbb{F}[\epsilon]
and suppose that

\widetilde{z}_{1}=(z_{1},t_{1})\in Z_{\operatorname{red}}(R_{1})\times T(R_{1})=\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}(R_{1})
corresponds to an element of
the kernel of the induced map

{\operatorname{Tan}}_{\widetilde{z}}(\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}})={\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z_{\operatorname{red}})\times{\operatorname{Tan}}_{t}(T)\to{\operatorname{Tan}}_{z}(Z),

i.e., that the triple
(\underline{\widetilde{A}}_{1}^{\prime},\underline{\widetilde{A}}_{1},\psi_{1})
associated to

\widetilde{z}_{1}
is (isomorphic to) the pull-back of the triple
(\underline{A}_{0},\underline{A}_{0}^{\prime},\psi_{0})

corresponding to
z
. The fact that
Z_{\operatorname{red}}\hookrightarrow Z
is a closed immersion then implies that
z_{1}
is trivial,
and the fact that
\widetilde{F}_{\tau,1}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}
corresponds to
F_{\tau,0}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}

under the canonical isomorphism
H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widetilde{A}^{\prime}_{1}/R_{1})\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}_{0}/\mathbb{F})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}

implies that
\widetilde{L}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta,1}
corresponds to
L_{\sigma^{-1}\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}
under
the isomorphisms
\widetilde{P}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta,1}\cong P^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta,0}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}\cong P_{\theta,0}^{(p^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}R_{1}
, and hence that
t_{1}
is trivial.

Since
\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}\longrightarrow Z
is injective on tangent spaces, it is a closed immersion, and
the same argument as in the proof of
[
DKS23
, Lemma 7.2.3]
shows that it is an isomorphism.
Alternatively, note that the diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}&\longrightarrow&Z\\
{\scriptstyle{q}}\downarrow&&\downarrow\\
Z_{\operatorname{red}}&\longrightarrow&{\displaystyle\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}}\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0})\end{array}

commutes, where the right vertical map is the fibre of
\widetilde{\xi}_{J}
over
x
and the
bottom horizontal arrow is its restriction, i.e. (
29
). Recall from the proof of Lemma
4.5.1

that these morphisms are finite flat of degree
p^{n}
and
p^{n^{\prime}}
, respectively. Since
q

is finite flat of degree
p^{\delta}
, the composite morphism
\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}\to Z\to\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0})

is finite flat of degree
p^{n}
, and since
\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}\to Z
is a closed immersion, it follows that it
must be an isomorphism.

Note in particular that the above construction extends the isomorphisms
\delta_{\theta}
of (
31
)
and (
32
) to isomorphisms

{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta,0}^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}q^{*}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}\cong\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}

over
\widetilde{Z}_{\operatorname{red}}\cong Z
for all
\theta\in J
. We thus obtain the following extension of Theorem
4.6.2
,
maintaining the same notation except that now we write
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{\theta}\subset{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
for the vector bundles

{\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j)}/{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime(j-1)}
associated to the data
\underline{A}^{\prime}
, where

\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
,
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
and
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)
is the universal triple over
Z
,
and we let

(33)

\varepsilon_{\theta}:{{\mathcal{P}}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta,0}^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}

denote the inverse of the isomorphism just constructed over
Z
.

Theorem 4.7.1
.

The morphism

Z\longrightarrow\displaystyle\prod_{\sigma^{-1}\theta\in J}\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0}^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})}),

defined so the tautological line bundle on
\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0}^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})})
pulls back
to
\varepsilon_{\theta}({\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta})
, is a closed immersion, identifying
Z
with the
fibre over
(L_{\theta,0})_{\theta\in J-J^{\prime}}
of the morphism

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\displaystyle\prod_{\sigma^{-1}\theta\in J}\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0}^{(n_{\theta}^{-1})})&\longrightarrow&\displaystyle\prod_{\theta\in J-J^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}(P_{\theta,0})\\
(M_{\theta})_{\theta}&\mapsto&(M_{\theta}^{(n_{\theta})})_{\theta}.\end{array}

We remark that the constructions in the proof of the theorem are independent of the quasi-polarizations in the data,
and hence compatible with the action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{F,(p),+}^{\times}
on the various schemes and vector bundles.
One therefore obtains an identical description of the geometric fibres of the morphism
\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})_{J}\to\overline{Y}_{J^{\prime}}

for sufficiently small
U
, with
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}\subset{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}
(resp.
L_{\theta,0}\subset P_{\theta,0}
)
replaced by restrictions (resp. fibres) of descents to
\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})_{J,\mathbb{F}}
(resp.
\overline{Y}_{J^{\prime},\mathbb{F}}
).
Furthermore the resulting descriptions are compatible with the action of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
in the obvious sense.
Since we will not make use of this, we leave it to the interested reader to make precise, and simply record the following
cruder, more immediate consequence of Theorem
4.7.1
, generalizing Theorem D of
[
DKS23
]
.

Corollary 4.7.2
.

Every geometric fibre of the morphism
\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})_{J}\to\overline{Y}_{J^{\prime}}

is isomorphic to
(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{F}})^{m}\times({\operatorname{Spec}\,}(\mathbb{F}[T]/T^{p}))^{\delta}
where
m
and
\delta
are as in
Lemma
4.5.1
, i.e.,
m=|J^{\prime}|=|J^{\prime\prime}|
and
\delta=|\Delta|=\#\{\,\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\,|\,\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}},\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i+1,1}\in J\,\}
.

5.
Cohomological vanishing

5.1.
Level
U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})

One of our main results will be the vanishing of the higher direct images of both the structure and dualizing
sheaves of
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
under the projection to
Y_{U}
. We will prove this also holds with
U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})

replaced by its open compact subgroup
U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}}):=\{\,g\in U\,|\,\mbox{$g_{\mathfrak{p}}\in I_{1}({\mathfrak{p}})$ for all ${\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}$}\,\}
, where

I_{1}({\mathfrak{p}})=\left\{\,\left.\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a&b\\
c&d\end{array}\right)\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}})\,\right|\,c,d-1\in{\mathfrak{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}\,\right\}.

We first recall the definition of suitable integral models for Hilbert modular varieties of level
U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
, following
[
Pap95
]
.
We consider the functor which, to a locally Noetherian
{\mathcal{O}}
-scheme
S
, associates the set of isomorphism classes of data

(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi,P)
, where
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi,P)
corresponds to an element of

\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}(S)
, and
P\in A(S)
is an
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})
-generator of
\ker(\psi)
.
The functor is represented by an
{\mathcal{O}}
-scheme which we denote
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
, and the forgetful
morphism
\widetilde{f}:\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
is finite flat.

The scheme
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
can be described explicitly as follows in terms of
H=\ker(\psi)
,
where
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)
is the universal triple over
S:=\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
. Firstly, the same argument
as when
p
is unramified in
F
shows that
H
is a Raynaud
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})
-module scheme (in the sense that

H[{\mathfrak{p}}]
is a Raynaud
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{p}})
-vector space scheme for each
{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}
). The correspondence of

[
Ray74
, Thm. 1.4.1]
thus associates to
H
an invertible
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
-module
{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}
for each
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
, together with morphisms

s_{\tau}:{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}_{\phi\circ\tau}\quad\mbox{and}\quad t_{\tau}:{\mathcal{R}}_{\phi\circ\tau}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}

such that
s_{\tau}\circ t_{\tau}=w_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
for a certain fixed element
w_{\mathfrak{p}}\in p{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
.
We then have

H=\mathbf{Spec}((\operatorname{Sym}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{S}}{\mathcal{R}})/{\mathcal{I}}),

where
{\mathcal{R}}=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}
,

\alpha\in{\mathcal{O}}_{F}
acts on
{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}

as the Teichmüller lift of
\overline{\tau}(\alpha)
,
and
{\mathcal{I}}
is the sheaf of ideals generated by the
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
-submodules

(s_{\tau}-1){\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{\otimes p}
for
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
.
The comultiplication on
H

is given via duality by the scheme
structure on the Cartier dual

H^{\vee}=\mathbf{Spec}((\operatorname{Sym}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{S}}{\mathcal{R}}^{\vee})/{\mathcal{J}}),

where
{\mathcal{R}}^{\vee}=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{-1}
,
and
{\mathcal{J}}
is generated by

(t_{\tau}-1)({\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{-1})^{\otimes p}
for
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
, where

t_{\tau}
is regarded as a morphism
({\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{-1})^{\otimes p}\to{\mathcal{R}}_{\phi\circ\tau}^{-1}
.
The same argument as in
[
Pap95
, 5.1]
then shows that
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}

can be identified with the closed
subscheme of
H
defined by the sheaf of ideals generated by the

(s_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)\left(\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},0}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{\otimes(p-1)}\right)
for
{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}
,
where
s_{\mathfrak{p}}:=\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},0}}s_{\tau}
is viewed as a morphism

\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},0}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{\otimes(p-1)}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
.

Note that the scheme
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
is equipped with a natural action of

({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})^{\times}
over
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
, defined by
\alpha\cdot(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi,P)=(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi,\alpha P)
. Furthermore we may write

(34)

\widetilde{f}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}}=\bigoplus_{\chi}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}

where
\chi
runs over all characters
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})^{\times}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
, and each summand may be
written as

{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}=\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{\otimes m_{\chi,\tau}}

where the integers
m_{\tau,\chi}
are uniquely determined by the conditions

•

0\leq m_{\chi,\tau}\leq p-1
for all
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
;

•

m_{\chi,\tau}<p-1
for some
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},0}
for each
{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}
;

•

\overline{\chi}(\alpha)=\prod_{\tau}\overline{\tau}(\alpha)^{m_{\chi,\tau}}
for all
\alpha\in({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})^{\times}
.

Since
\widetilde{f}
is finite flat, we may similarly decompose the direct image of the dualizing sheaf as

(35)

\widetilde{f}_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}={\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{S}}(\widetilde{f}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}},{\mathcal{K}}_{S/{\mathcal{O}}})=\bigoplus_{\chi}{\mathcal{H}om}({\mathcal{R}}_{\chi},{\mathcal{K}}_{S/{\mathcal{O}}}).

Assuming as usual that
U
is sufficiently small, and in particular
\alpha-1\in{\mathfrak{P}}
for all

\alpha\in U\cap F^{\times}
, the action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{F,(p),+}^{\times}
on
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
via
multiplication on quasi-polarizations factors through a free action of

{\mathcal{O}}_{F,(p),+}^{\times}/(U\cap F^{\times})^{2}
, and we let
Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
denote the quotient scheme.
The morphism
\widetilde{f}
descends to a finite flat morphism

f:Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
, so
Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
is
Cohen–Macaulay and quasi-projective of relative dimension
d
over
{\mathcal{O}}
.
Furthermore for sufficiently small
U
,
U^{\prime}
of level prime to
p
and
g\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})

such that
g^{-1}Ug\subset U^{\prime}
, we obtain a finite étale
morphism
\tilde{\rho}_{g}:\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to\widetilde{Y}_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
, descending
to
{\rho}_{g}:{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to{Y}_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
and satisfying the usual compatibilities.

Note that the assumption on
U
implies that the
canonical isomorphism
\nu^{*}\underline{A}\cong\underline{A}
over
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}

is the identity on
H
for all
\nu\in(U\cap F^{\times})^{2}
, so
H
descends to a Raynaud

{\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}}
-module scheme on
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
, which we also denote by
H
. Furthermore
the line bundles
{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}
and morphisms
s_{\tau}
,
t_{\tau}

all descend to give the same descriptions of
H
,
H^{\vee}
and
Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}

as spectra of finite flat
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}}
-algebras (with
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})^{\times}
-actions)
over
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
, and we obtain analogous decompositions of
f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}}

and
f_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}
.

5.2.
Dicing

We will use the method of “dicing,” introduced in
[
DKS23
, §6.2]
, to reduce the proofs
of the cohomological vanishing results to consideration
of line bundles on irreducible components
19
19

19

We could alternatively have
used
\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
and
\overline{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})_{J}
, as in
[
DKS23
]
, but for the purpose of
proving the desired vanishing results, we can replace them by étale covers.

of the special fibre of
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
, or equivalently
of the schemes denoted
S_{J}
.

Recall from §
4.1
that for any subset
J\subset\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
,
the closed subscheme
S_{J}
of
\overline{S}:=\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}),k}
is defined by the vanishing of

\{\,\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}\,|\,\theta\in J\,\}\quad\cup\{\,\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}\,|\,\theta\not\in J\,\},

where
\psi:A\to A^{\prime}
is the universal isogeny over
\overline{S}
and

\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}:{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta},\qquad\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}:{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}

are the morphisms induced by
\psi
on the indicated subquotients of de Rham cohomology sheaves of

A
and
A^{\prime}
.

Following
[
DKS23
, §6.2]
, we consider the sheaves of ideals
{\mathcal{I}}_{j}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{S}}
corresponding
to the closed subscheme of
\overline{S}
defined by the vanishing of

\left\{\left.\,\bigotimes_{\theta\in J}\psi_{{\mathcal{L}},\theta}^{*}\,\right|\,J\subset\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}}},\,|J|=j\,\right\},

or equivalently the image of the morphism

\bigoplus_{|J|=j}\left(\bigotimes_{\theta\in J}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\,\longrightarrow\,{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{S}}

induced by the
\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}
. We thus have

{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{S}}={\mathcal{I}}_{0}\supset{\mathcal{I}}_{1}\supset{\mathcal{I}}_{2}\supset\cdots{\mathcal{I}}_{d_{\mathfrak{P}}}\supset{\mathcal{I}}_{1+d_{\mathfrak{P}}}=0,

where
d_{{\mathfrak{P}}}=|\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}}}|=\sum_{{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}}e_{\mathfrak{p}}f_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Letting
i_{J}
denote the closed immersion
S_{J}\hookrightarrow\overline{S}

and
{\mathcal{I}}_{J}
the sheaf of ideals on
S_{J}
defining the vanishing locus of
\bigotimes_{\theta\in J}\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}
,
we see exactly as in
[
DKS23
, §6.2.1]
that
{\mathcal{I}}_{J}
is invertible and
the proof of
[
DKS23
, Lemma 6.2.1]
carries over
mutatis mutandis
to give the following:

Lemma 5.2.1
.

The natural map
{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j}{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{S}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{|J|=j}i_{J,*}{\mathcal{I}}_{J}
is
an isomorphism for all
j=0,\ldots,d_{\mathfrak{P}}
.

Similarly, letting
{\mathcal{J}}_{J}
denote the (invertible) sheaf of ideals on
S_{J}
defining the vanishing locus of
20
20

20

Since

\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}:{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}
is an isomorphism for
\theta\not\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
, we may view the tensor
product as being over either
\Sigma-J
or
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}-J
, and we write simply
\theta\not\in J
.

\bigotimes_{\theta\not\in J}\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}
, the same argument as in
[
DKS23
, §6.2.2]
yields
the following description of the graded pieces of the induced filtration

{\operatorname{Fil}\,}^{j}{\mathcal{K}}_{\overline{S}/k}:={\mathcal{I}}_{j}{\mathcal{K}}_{\overline{S}/k}
on the dualizing sheaf:

Lemma 5.2.2
.

The natural map
{\operatorname{gr}\,}^{j}{\mathcal{K}}_{\overline{S}/k}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{|J|=j}i_{J,*}({\mathcal{J}}_{J}^{-1}{\mathcal{K}}_{S_{J}/k})
is
an isomorphism for all
j=0,\ldots,d_{\mathfrak{P}}
.

Furthermore letting
\overline{S}_{1}=\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}}),k}
, the decompositions (
34
) and
(
35
) restrict to give the decompositions

(36)

\widetilde{f}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{S}_{1}}=\bigoplus_{\chi}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{f}_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{\overline{S}_{1}/k}=\bigoplus_{\chi}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}^{-1}{\mathcal{K}}_{\overline{S}/k},

where we again write
\widetilde{f}:\overline{S}_{1}\to\overline{S}
for the natural projection and

{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}
for the Raynaud bundles associated to
H=\ker(\psi)
over
\overline{S}
.
Note also that we may now view the decompositions as being over characters

\chi:({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})^{\times}\to k^{\times}
.

Following the method of
[
DKS23
, §7.3]
will reduce the proof of the desired cohomological vanishing to a computation involving
the line bundles
{\mathcal{I}}_{J}
,
{\mathcal{J}}_{J}
,
{\mathcal{K}}_{{S}_{J}/k}
and (the pull-back of) the
{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}
over
S_{J}
.
We will now describe all of these in terms of the line bundles
{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}
,
{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}

and
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
.

We start with the Raynaud bundles
{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}
over
S_{J}
for
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
.
Suppose first that
\theta:=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\not\in J
, so that
\psi_{{\mathcal{L}},\theta}^{*}
vanishes on
S_{J}
,
and therefore

\psi_{\tau}^{*}:{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A^{\prime}/S_{J})_{\tau}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/S_{J})

maps
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau}
onto
{\mathcal{F}}^{e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1}_{\tau}

(where we are now writing
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)
for the universal object
over
S_{J}
). It follows that the cokernel of
(s^{\prime}_{*}\Omega^{1}_{A^{\prime}/S_{J}})_{\tau}\to(s_{*}\Omega^{1}_{A/S_{J}})_{\tau}
is
isomorphic to the invertible sheaf
{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}={\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}/{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}
on
S_{J}
, so

{\mathcal{L}ie}(H/S_{J})_{\tau}=\ker({\mathcal{L}ie}(A/S_{J})_{\tau}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}ie}(A^{\prime}/S_{J})_{\tau})

is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{-1}
, and therefore
i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}\cong{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}

(as in the proof of
[
DKS23
, Lem. 5.1.1]
).

Similarly if
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\in J
, then
{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau}
is the preimage of

{\mathcal{F}}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}_{\tau}
under
\psi_{\tau}^{*}
, so the kernel of
R^{1}s^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{A^{\prime}}\to R^{1}s_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{A}

is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}={\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}/{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{\prime(e_{\mathfrak{p}})}
.
It follows that

{\mathcal{L}ie}(H^{\vee}/S_{J})_{\tau}\cong\ker({\mathcal{L}ie}((A^{\prime})^{\vee}/S_{J})_{\tau}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}ie}(A^{\vee}/S_{J})_{\tau})

is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
, and therefore so is
i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}
. Summing up, we have constructed
a canonical isomorphism

(37)

i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}\,\,\cong\,\,\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta},&\mbox{if $\theta\not\in J$.}\\
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}&\mbox{if $\theta\in J$},\end{array}\right.

where
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
and
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

Next considering
{\mathcal{I}}_{J}
, its definition (and invertibility) imply that
\bigotimes_{\theta\in J}\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}
induces
an isomorphism

(38)

\bigotimes_{\theta\in J}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\,\,\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\,\,{\mathcal{I}}_{J}.

Similarly, we have that

\bigotimes_{\theta\not\in J}\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{M}}}^{*}
induces
an isomorphism

(39)

\bigotimes_{\theta\not\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{-1}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\,\,\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\,\,{\mathcal{J}}_{J},

but we will need to further relate the
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
(for
\theta\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}}}-J
) to other line bundles on
S_{J}
.

Let
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
for some
{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}
, and let
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
,
and suppose first that
\theta,\sigma^{-1}\theta\not\in J
.
If
j>1
, then consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\theta}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}.}

Note that the image of
u:{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\prime}
is
{\mathcal{L}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\prime}=\ker(\psi_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{*})

(since
\sigma^{-1}\theta\not\in J
), so the image of
\psi_{\theta}^{*}
is contained in the kernel of

u:{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}
, which is
{\mathcal{M}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}={\mathcal{G}}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}/{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{(j-1)}
.
Since each is a rank one subbundle of
{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}
, it follows that

{\mathcal{M}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}={\operatorname{im}}(\psi_{\theta}^{*})\cong{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{\theta}/\ker(\psi_{\theta}^{*})={\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}^{\prime}/{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\prime}={\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}.

Similarly if
j=1
, then we consider instead the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\theta}^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\prime(p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\sigma^{-1}\theta^{*(p)}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{P}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{(p)},}

where the vertical maps are defined by the partial Hasse invariant (see (
16
) and (
17
)).
The same reasoning as above then shows that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
is isomorphic to the kernel of

\widetilde{h}_{\theta}:{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}^{(p)}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta},

which in this case is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{M}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{(p)}
, via the morphism
induced by the restriction of
{\operatorname{Frob}}^{*}
to

({\mathcal{G}}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}})})^{(p)}=u^{-1}({\mathcal{F}}^{(e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\tau})^{(p)}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(A/S_{J})_{\tau}[u]={\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}.

Suppose on the other hand that
\theta\not\in J
, but
\sigma^{-1}\theta\in J
, so
\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}
.
Since
\underline{A}^{\prime\prime}
corresponds to an element of
T_{J^{\prime\prime}}(S_{J})
, we have that the kernel of

\widetilde{h}^{\prime}_{\theta}:{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{\theta}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime(n_{\theta})}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}

is
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{\theta}
, and hence that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime(n_{\theta})}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}
.

Summing up, we have shown that if
\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}-J
, then there is a canonical isomorphism

(40)

{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\,\,\cong\,\,\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{M}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{(n_{\theta})},&\mbox{if $\sigma^{-1}\theta\not\in J$}\\
{\mathcal{L}}_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}^{\prime(n_{\theta})},&\mbox{if $\sigma^{-1}\theta\in J$}.\end{array}\right.

Finally, although we will not need the following description of the line bundles
{\mathcal{K}}_{S_{J}/k}
, we provide it
for completeness and coherence with the theme of Kodaira–Spencer isomorphisms. Recall that the
isomorphism of Theorem
3.2.1
arises from a canonical isomorphism

{\mathcal{K}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}\,\,\cong\,\,\bigotimes_{\theta\in\Sigma}\left(\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}}_{\theta}^{\prime-1}\widetilde{{\mathcal{L}}}_{\theta}\right)

(using
\widetilde{\cdot}
for universal data over
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
). On the other
hand the proof of Lemma
5.2.2
(see
[
DKS23
, §6.2.2]
) also gives a canonical isomorphism

{\mathcal{K}}_{S_{J}/k}\,\,\cong\,\,{\mathcal{I}}_{J}{\mathcal{J}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{\overline{S}/k}.

Combining these with (
38
) and (
39
), we obtain a canonical isomorphism

(41)

{\mathcal{K}}_{S_{J}/k}\,\,\cong\,\,\left(\bigotimes_{\theta\in\Sigma-J}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}\right){\textstyle\bigotimes}\left(\bigotimes_{\theta\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\prime}\right).

(Recall that for
\theta\not\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}
, we have
{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}\cong{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}\cong{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
,
so these factors could just as well have been included with
\theta\in J
instead of
\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}-J
.)

We remark that (
41
) can also be proved more directly using a deformation-theoretic argument, which
furthermore produces a filtration on
\Omega^{1}_{S_{J}/k}
analogous to the one on
\Omega^{1}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}
defined
by Reduzzi and Xiao in
[
RX17
, §2.8]
(or more precisely
[
Dia23
, §3.3]
for the current set-up).

We remark also that the various isomorphisms of line bundles established in this section should be compatible
in the usual sense with descent data and Hecke action, but we will not need this for the purpose of proving the
desired cohomological vanishing theorems.

5.3.
Vanishing, duality and flatness results

We are now ready to prove the main result, which is a generalization of Theorem E of
[
DKS23
]
.
Indeed all the ingredients are now in place to apply the same argument as in
[
DKS23
, §7.3]
.
Recall that
U
is any sufficiently small open compact subgroup of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F,p})
,
{\mathfrak{P}}

is the product of any set of primes of
{\mathcal{O}}_{F}
dividing
p
, and

Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}Y_{U}

are the natural degeneracy maps induced by the forgetful morphisms

\widetilde{f}:\widetilde{Y}_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
and
\widetilde{\pi}_{1}:\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to\widetilde{Y}_{U}
.
We will write simply
Y
(resp.
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
,
Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
) for
Y_{U}
(resp.
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
,
Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
) when
Y
is fixed,
and similarly abbreviate
\widetilde{Y}_{U}
, etc.
We let
\widetilde{\varphi}
(resp.
\varphi
) denote the composite
\widetilde{\pi}_{1}\circ\widetilde{f}
(resp.
\pi_{1}\circ f
).

Theorem 5.3.1
.

The higher direct image sheaves

R^{i}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad R^{i}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}

vanish for all
i>0
.

Proof.
First note that since
\widetilde{Y}\to Y
is étale and
\widetilde{Y}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})=\widetilde{Y}\times_{Y}Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
, we may replace
Y
(resp.
Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
,
\varphi
)
by
\widetilde{Y}
(resp.
\widetilde{Y}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
,
\widetilde{\varphi}
). Next note that since

\widetilde{\varphi}
is projective and
\widetilde{\varphi}_{K}
is finite, we may replace these in turn
by their special fibres.
Furthermore since
\widetilde{f}
is finite, we have

R^{i}\widetilde{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{S}_{1}}=R^{i}\widetilde{\pi}_{1,*}(\widetilde{f}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{S}_{1}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad R^{i}\widetilde{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{\overline{S}_{1}/k}=R^{i}\widetilde{\pi}_{1,*}(\widetilde{f}_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{\overline{S}_{1}/k})

(where we again let
\overline{S}_{1}=\widetilde{Y}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})_{k}
and
suppress the subscript
k
from the notation for the morphisms).
Using the decompositions given in (
35
) and the filtrations whose graded pieces are
described in Lemmas
5.2.1
and
5.2.2
, we are therefore reduced to proving the vanishing of

R^{i}\pi_{J,*}({\mathcal{I}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi})\quad\mbox{and}\quad R^{i}\pi_{J,*}(({\mathcal{J}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi})^{-1}{\mathcal{K}}_{S_{J}/k})

for all
i>0
,
J\subset\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}
and
\chi:({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})^{\times}\to k^{\times}
, where
i_{J}
is the closed
immersion
S_{J}\hookrightarrow\overline{S}
and
\pi_{J}:S_{J}\to T_{J^{\prime}}
is the restriction of
\widetilde{\pi}_{1}
.

Let us now fix
J
and
\chi
, and write
\chi=\prod\overline{\tau}^{m_{\tau}}
, where the product is over

\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{P}},0}
,
0\leq m_{\tau}\leq p-1
for each
\tau
, and
m_{\tau}<p-1
for some
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},0}

for each
{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}
. We now apply the results of the preceding section to write the line bundles

{\mathcal{I}}_{J}
,
{\mathcal{J}}_{J}
and
i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}
on
S_{J}
in terms of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}
,
{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
.

Firstly by formula (
37
), we have

(42)

i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}\cong\left(\bigotimes_{\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}-J}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\otimes m_{\theta}}\right){\textstyle\bigotimes}\left(\bigotimes_{\theta\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime\otimes m_{\theta}}\right),

where
m_{\theta}=m_{\tau}
if
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}
, and
m_{\theta}=0

if
\theta=\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
for some
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Combining (
42
) with (
38
) and the isomorphisms

{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{\theta}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{\theta}={\mathcal{N}}^{\prime}_{\theta}\cong{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}={\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}

(see Remark
3.1.4
), we obtain an isomorphism

(43)

{\mathcal{I}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}\cong\left(\bigotimes_{\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}-J}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\otimes m_{\theta}}\right){\textstyle\bigotimes}\left(\bigotimes_{\theta\in J}({\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime\otimes(m_{\theta}-1)}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta})\right).

On the other hand combining (
42
) with (
39
) and (
40
) gives

\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathcal{J}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}\cong\left(\displaystyle\bigotimes_{\theta,\sigma\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}-J}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\otimes(n_{\sigma\theta}-1)}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\otimes m_{\theta}}\right)\bigotimes\left(\displaystyle\bigotimes_{\theta\not\in J,\sigma\theta\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\otimes m_{\theta}}\right)\\
\bigotimes\left(\displaystyle\bigotimes_{\theta\in J,\sigma\theta\not\in J}({\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime\otimes m_{\theta}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\prime\otimes n_{\sigma\theta}})\right)\bigotimes\left(\displaystyle\bigotimes_{\theta,\sigma\theta\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime\otimes m_{\theta}}\right).\end{array}

Combined with the isomorphisms
{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}\cong{\mathcal{N}}^{\prime}_{\theta}
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}^{\otimes n_{\sigma\theta}}\cong{\mathcal{N}}_{\sigma\theta}

(see the last paragraph of
[
Dia23
, §4.1]
for the latter), this simplifies to

(44)

\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathcal{J}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}\cong\left(\displaystyle\bigotimes_{\theta,\sigma\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}}-J}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\otimes(m_{\theta}-n_{\sigma\theta}+1)}\right)\bigotimes\left(\displaystyle\bigotimes_{\theta\not\in J,\sigma\theta\in J}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\otimes(m_{\theta}+1)}\right)\\
\bigotimes\left(\displaystyle\bigotimes_{\theta\in J,\sigma\theta\not\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime\otimes(m_{\theta}-n_{\sigma\theta})}\right)\bigotimes\left(\displaystyle\bigotimes_{\theta,\sigma\theta\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime\otimes m_{\theta}}\right).\end{array}

Since
\pi_{J}
is flat and projective, it suffices to prove the vanishing of cohomology of fibres, i.e., that

H^{i}(Z,j^{*}({\mathcal{I}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}))=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad H^{i}(Z,j^{*}(({\mathcal{J}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi})^{-1}{\mathcal{K}}_{S_{J}/k})))=0

for all
x\in T_{J^{\prime}}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
, where
j:Z\to S_{J}
is the closed immersion of the fibre of
\pi_{J}
over
x
. Furthermore since
\pi_{J}
is Cohen-Macaulay,
we have
j^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{S_{J}/k}={\mathcal{K}}_{Z/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
. Since the line bundles
j^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}=L_{\theta,x}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
and
j^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}=M_{\theta,x}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
are trivial
on
Z
, the isomorphisms (
43
) and (
44
) give

(45)

j^{*}({\mathcal{I}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi})\cong\bigotimes_{\theta\in J}j^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime\otimes(m_{\theta}-1)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad j^{*}({\mathcal{J}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi})^{-1}\cong\bigotimes_{\theta\in J}j^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime\otimes(\ell_{\theta})},

where
\ell_{\theta}=n_{\sigma\theta}-m_{\theta}
if
\sigma\theta\not\in J
and
\ell_{\theta}=-m_{\theta}
if
\sigma\theta\in J
.

We now appeal to the description of
Z
in Theorem
4.7.1
, which provides an isomorphism

Z\,\,\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\,\,{\operatorname{Spec}\,}C\times\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{1}

for a finite (Gorenstein)
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
-algebra
C
, such that if
\theta\in J
, then

j^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}^{\prime}
corresponds to the pull-back of
{\mathcal{O}}(1)_{\sigma\theta}

(resp. an invertible
C
-module) if
\sigma\theta\not\in J
(resp.
\sigma\theta\in J
).
It therefore follows from (
45
) that

H^{i}(Z,\,j^{*}({\mathcal{I}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}))\cong H^{i}\left(\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{1},\bigotimes_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}{\mathcal{O}}(m_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}-1)_{\theta}\right)\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}C,

which vanishes since
m_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}\geq 0
for all
\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}
. Similarly since
{\mathcal{K}}_{Z/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
corresponds to
the dualizing sheaf of

{\operatorname{Spec}\,}C\times\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, which is isomorphic to
\otimes_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}{\mathcal{O}}(-2)_{\theta}
, we have

H^{i}(Z,\,j^{*}(({\mathcal{J}}_{J}i_{J}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi})^{-1}{\mathcal{K}}_{S_{J}/k})))\cong H^{i}\left(\prod_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{1},\bigotimes_{\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}}{\mathcal{O}}(\ell_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}-2)_{\theta}\right)\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}C,

which vanishes since
\ell_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}=n_{\theta}-m_{\sigma^{-1}\theta}\geq 1
for all
\theta\in J^{\prime\prime}
.

\square

Corollary 5.3.2
.

The higher direct image sheaves

R^{i}\pi_{j,*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad R^{i}\pi_{j,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}

vanish for all
i>0
and
j=1,2
.

Proof.
As in the proof of the theorem, we may replace
\pi_{j}
by

\widetilde{\pi}_{j}:\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})\to\widetilde{Y}
.
By formula (
34
), the line bundle

{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
is a direct summand of
\widetilde{f}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
,
so the vanishing of

R^{i}\widetilde{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}=R^{i}\widetilde{\pi}_{1,*}(\widetilde{f}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})})

for all
i>0
implies that of
R^{i}\widetilde{\pi}_{1,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
.
Similarly the vanishing of
R^{i}\widetilde{\pi}_{1,*}{\mathcal{K}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}
follows from
(
35
) and the theorem.

For the analogous assertions for
\widetilde{\pi}_{2}
, note that there is an automorphism

\widetilde{w}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
of
\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
such that

\widetilde{\pi}_{2}=\widetilde{\pi}_{1}\circ\widetilde{w}_{\mathfrak{P}}
. Indeed define
\widetilde{w}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
by the triple

(\underline{A}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}\underline{A},\xi)
, where
(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{\prime},\psi)
is the
universal triple over
\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
,
{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}\underline{A}
denotes
{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}A

endowed with the evident auxiliary data, in particular quasi-polarization

\varpi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{2}\otimes\lambda:{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}A\to{\mathfrak{P}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}A^{\vee}=({\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}A)^{\vee},

and
\xi:A^{\prime}\to{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}A
is such that
\xi\circ\psi
is the
canonical isogeny
A\to{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}A
. The desired vanishing of higher direct images under
\widetilde{\pi}_{2}

therefore follows from the corresponding assertions for
\widetilde{\pi}_{1}
and the identifications

\widetilde{w}_{{\mathfrak{P}},*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}={\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
and

\widetilde{w}_{{\mathfrak{P}},*}{\mathcal{K}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}={\mathcal{K}}_{\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}

\square

Remark 5.3.3
.

We caution that the automorphism
w_{\mathfrak{P}}
of
\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
does
not
lift to
an automorphism of
\widetilde{Y}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
, so the argument handling the case of
\pi_{2}
in
in the proof of the corollary cannot be used to deduce of vanishing of higher direct images under
\pi_{2}\circ f

from the theorem.

Corollary 5.3.4
.

The direct image sheaves
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}
and
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
are locally free
of rank
\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}}(p^{2f_{\mathfrak{p}}}-1)
over
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
. Furthermore there is a perfect Hecke-equivariant
21
21

21

In the sense that diagram (
48
) in the proof commutes.
pairing

\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}\,\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}\,\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}\,\longrightarrow\,{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}.

Proof.
We apply Grothendieck–Serre duality to the proper morphism
\varphi
. Since
R^{i}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}

vanishes for all
i>0
, the duality isomorphism of
[
StaX
, §0AU3(4c)]
(taking
K
there to be
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}[d]
) degenerates to

R\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}\,\,\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\,\,R{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})},{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}).

In particular we obtain an isomorphism

(46)

\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}\,\,\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\,\,{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})},{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}).

Furthermore since
R^{i}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}
vanishes for all
i>0
, it follows that so does

{\mathcal{E}xt}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}^{i}(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})},{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}})
, and hence

{\mathcal{E}xt}^{i}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})},{\mathcal{O}}_{Y})=0

for all
i>0
. Since
Y
is regular, it follows that
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
is locally free,
with rank given by the degree of the finite morphism
\varphi_{K}
, namely

[U:U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})]=\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}}[\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}):I_{1}({\mathfrak{p}})]=\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}}(p^{2f_{\mathfrak{p}}}-1).

The same argument with the roles of
{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
exchanged
shows that
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}
is locally free of the same rank.

Furthermore it follows from the construction of the duality isomorphism that (
46
) is induced by
the trace morphism denoted
{\operatorname{Tr}\,}_{\varphi,{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}}
in
[
StaX
, §0AWG]
, which may be viewed as
a morphism

(47)
