{\operatorname{Tr}\,}:\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}

since
R^{i}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}
vanishes for all
i>0
.

Suppose then that
U
and
U^{\prime}
are sufficiently small and that
g\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
is such that

g^{-1}U^{\prime}g\subset U^{\prime}
, giving étale morphisms
\rho_{g}:Y_{U^{\prime}}\to Y_{U}
and

\rho_{g}^{\prime}:Y_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
such that the diagram

\textstyle{Y_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{g}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{Y_{U^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{g}}
\textstyle{Y_{U}}

is Cartesian, and identifications
\rho_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}={\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}}/{\mathcal{O}}}
and

\rho_{g}^{\prime*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}={\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}
. The commutativity of the
resulting diagram

\textstyle{\rho_{g}^{*}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{g}^{*}({\operatorname{Tr}\,})}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\rho_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi^{\prime}_{*}\rho^{\prime*}_{g}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{Tr}\,}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}}/{\mathcal{O}}}}

then follows from
[
StaX
, Lemma 0B6J]
and implies that of

\textstyle{\rho_{g}^{*}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}}}}(\rho_{g}^{*}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}},\rho_{g}^{*}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}}}}(\rho_{g}^{*}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}},\rho_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U}/{\mathcal{O}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}}}}(\rho_{g}^{*}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}},\varphi^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}}}}(\rho_{g}^{*}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}},{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}}/{\mathcal{O}}})}
\textstyle{\varphi^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}}}}(\varphi^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}},\varphi^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}/{\mathcal{O}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}}}}(\varphi^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}},{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{U^{\prime}}/{\mathcal{O}}}),\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

where the vertical maps are base-change isomorphisms, the horizontal maps on the left are the canonical morphisms, and the ones on the right are composition with
\rho_{g}^{*}({\operatorname{Tr}\,})
or
{\operatorname{Tr}\,}^{\prime}
.
This in turn gives the
Hecke-equivariance of the pairing, i.e.,
the commutativity of

(48)

where the top (resp. bottom) horizontal arrow is induced by
{\operatorname{Tr}\,}
(resp.

) and the vertical maps
are the canonical isomorphisms.

\square

Recall that for sufficiently small
U
, the finite flat Raynaud
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})
-module scheme
H
over
\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})

descends to
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
, and hence so do the decompositions (
34
) and (
35
), giving

f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}=\bigoplus_{\chi}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}\quad\mbox{and}\quad f_{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}=\bigoplus_{\chi}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}^{-1}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}},

where the line bundles
{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}
(now on
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
) are indexed by the
characters
\chi:({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})^{\times}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
. Theorem
5.3.1
thus implies
the vanishing of
R^{i}\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}
and
R^{i}\pi_{1,*}({\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}^{-1}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}})

for all
i>0
and characters
\chi
, and Corollary
5.3.4
implies that

\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}
and
\pi_{1,*}({\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}^{-1}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}})

are locally free, now of rank
\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}}(p^{f_{\mathfrak{p}}}+1)
. Furthermore the
same argument as in the proof of its Hecke-equivariance shows that the pairing defined
in the corollary is compatible with the natural action of
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathfrak{P}})^{\times}
on
Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
,
and hence respects the decompositions and restricts to perfect Hecke-equivariant pairings

\pi_{1,*}({\mathcal{R}}_{\chi^{-1}}^{-1}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}})\,\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}\,\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}

for all characters
\chi
. We record this in the case of the trivial character:

Corollary 5.3.5
.

The direct image sheaves
\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}
and
\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}
are locally free
of rank
\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}}(p^{f_{\mathfrak{p}}}+1)
over
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
, and there is a perfect Hecke-equivariant pairing

\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}\,\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}\,\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})}\,\longrightarrow\,{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}.

Remark 5.3.6
.

The same argument as in the proof of Corollary
5.3.2
shows that we can replace

\pi_{1}
by
\pi_{2}
in the statement of Corollary
5.3.5
. (In fact the automorphism
\widetilde{w}_{\mathfrak{P}}

descends to an automorphism
w_{\mathfrak{P}}
of
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
such that
\pi_{2}=\pi_{1}\circ w_{\mathfrak{P}}
.) Note however that
this argument does not allow one to replace
\pi_{1}
by
\pi_{2}
in the assertions for the twists by the bundles
{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}

(cf. Remark
5.3.3
).

Suppose now that
R
is any (Noetherian)
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra and consider the base extensions
from
{\mathcal{O}}
to
R
of the schemes
Y
and
Y_{i}({\mathfrak{P}})
for
i=0,1
and morphisms
\varphi
,
\pi_{j}

for
j=1,2
, which we denote
Y_{R}
, etc. We then have:

Corollary 5.3.7
.

For
{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})}
and
{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})/{\mathcal{O}}}
,
the base-change morphisms

(R^{i}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{E}})_{R}\to R^{i}\varphi_{R,*}({\mathcal{E}}_{R})

are isomorphisms for all
i\geq 0
. In particular,
R^{i}\varphi_{R,*}({\mathcal{E}}_{R})=0
for all
i>0
,

\varphi_{R,*}({\mathcal{E}}_{R})
is locally free over
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{R}}
of rank
\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}|{\mathfrak{P}}}(p^{2f_{\mathfrak{p}}}-1)
,
and there is a perfect Hecke-equivariant pairing

\varphi_{R,*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})_{R}/R}\,\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{R}}}\,\varphi_{R,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})_{R}}\,\longrightarrow\,{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{R}/R}.

Furthermore analogous assertions hold with
U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
replaced by
U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
and
\varphi

replaced by
\pi_{j}
for
j=1
and
2
.

Proof.
Since
Y
and
Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
are flat over
{\mathcal{O}}
, the schemes
Y_{R}
and
Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})

are Tor independent over
Y
(in the sense of
[
StaX
, Defn. 08IA]
). The assertions in the case
of
U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
are then immediate from
[
StaX
, Lem. 08IB]
, Theorem
5.3.1
,
Corollary
5.3.4
, and the compatibility with base-change of formation of dualizing sheaves for
Cohen–Macaulay morphisms (see
[
Con00
, Thm. 3.6.1]
or Lemmas 0E2Y
and 0E9W of
[
StaX
]
). The same argument applies for
U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
, but using
Corollaries
5.3.2
and
5.3.5
and Remark
5.3.6
.

\square

Remark 5.3.8
.

Our method also yields an improvement on the

, Prop. 3.19]
. More precisely, suppose that
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}

and consider the automorphic line bundle

on

(as defined in §

).
Applying the same analysis as above to the line bundle

\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m}}

on

{\mathcal{R}}_{\chi}
,
one obtains a filtration on
\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},k}
for which the graded pieces have fibres (relative to

) described exactly as in the first formula
of (

), but with the exponents
22
22

22

Note that the

in (

) are not the constituents of the
weight vector
\mathbf{m}
appearing in §

.

m_{\theta}
replaced by

. (This refines a similar analysis carried out in

, §4.9]

; see Remark

.) The same argument as in the proof of

therefore
shows that if
n_{\sigma\theta}k_{\sigma\theta}\geq k_{\theta}

for all

, then

R^{i}\widetilde{\pi}_{1,*}(\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m}})
vanishes

23

The assertion in

]

for all

It follows also that if

We may also use the results above to construct integral models for Hilbert modular varieties of levels of

U_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
and
U_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
which are finite and flat over the smooth models of level
U
. Indeed consider
the Stein factorizations

Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})\to Y^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})\to Y\quad\mbox{and}\quad Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})\to Y^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})\to Y,

where
Y^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}}):=\mathbf{Spec}(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})})
and
Y^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}):=\mathbf{Spec}(\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})})
.
Note that
Y_{i}({\mathfrak{P}})_{K}=Y^{\prime}_{i}({\mathfrak{P}})_{K}
for
i=1,2
and that Corollaries
5.3.4
and
5.3.5

immediately imply the following:

Corollary 5.3.9
.

The schemes
Y^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
and
Y^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})
are finite and flat over
Y
; in particular
they are Cohen–Macaulay over
{\mathcal{O}}
.

Note that the same conclusions apply to
Y^{\prime\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}}):=\mathbf{Spec}(\pi_{2,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})})
; in fact
w_{\mathfrak{P}}

induces an isomorphism
Y^{\prime\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}Y^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
over
Y
. Note also that the natural
map
Y^{\prime}_{1}({\mathfrak{P}})\to Y
factors through
Y^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
. Furthermore if
U
,
U^{\prime}
are sufficiently small and

g\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
is such that
g^{-1}U^{\prime}g\subset U
, then (with the obvious notation) the morphisms

\rho_{g}:Y_{U^{\prime}_{i}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to Y_{U_{i}({\mathfrak{P}})}
for
i=0,1
induce morphisms
\rho_{g}^{\prime}:Y^{\prime}_{U^{\prime}_{i}({\mathfrak{P}})}\to Y^{\prime}_{U_{i}({\mathfrak{P}})}

satisfying the usual compatibilities.

Finally, we note the following consequence of Corollary
5.3.9
, pointed out to us by G. Pappas.

Corollary 5.3.10
.

The normalization of
Y
in
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})_{K}
relative to
\pi_{1}
(or to
\pi_{2}
) is flat over
Y
.

Proof.
First recall that
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
is normal, since for example it is regular in codimension one and
Cohen–Macaulay. Therefore it follows from
[
StaX
, Lem. 035L]
that
Y^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
is normal.
Since
Y^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
is also finite over
Y
, it is the normalization of
Y
in
Y^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})_{K}=Y_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})_{K}

(relative to
\pi_{1}
), and its flatness over
Y_{U}
follows from Corollary
5.3.9
.

The same argument applies with
\pi_{1}
(resp.
Y^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
) replaced by
\pi_{2}
(resp.
Y^{\prime\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{P}})
).

\square

5.4.
Hecke operators

We now specialize to the case of
{\mathfrak{P}}={\mathfrak{p}}
and consider the morphisms

\pi_{1},\pi_{2}:Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})\to Y,

Y
(resp.
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})
) for
Y_{U}
(resp.
Y_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})}
) when
U
is fixed.

The pairing of Corollary
5.3.5
thus defines an isomorphism

(49)

\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}(\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})/{\mathcal{O}}},\,{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}),

under which the image of the unit section is the trace morphism

(50)

{\operatorname{Tr}\,}:\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})/{\mathcal{O}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}

(defined as in (
47
) since
R^{i}\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})/{\mathcal{O}}}=0
for all
i>0
).
On the other hand we have the morphism
\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})/{\mathcal{O}}}
defined in (
13
),
which by Proposition
3.2.3
extends the canonical isomorphism over
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{K}
. It follows that
the composite of its direct image with
{\operatorname{Tr}\,}
,

(51)

\pi_{1,*}\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}\longrightarrow\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})/{\mathcal{O}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}},

extends the trace morphism over
Y_{K}
(associated to the finite flat morphism
\pi_{1,K}
). Thus if
{\mathcal{F}}

is any locally free sheaf over
Y
, we obtain an extension of the trace over
Y_{K}
to a morphism

{\operatorname{tr}\,}_{{\mathcal{F}}}:\pi_{1,*}\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}

by tensoring (
51
) with
{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}^{-1}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}
and applying the projection formula.

Recall that we have the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism
{\mathcal{K}}_{Y/{\mathcal{O}}}\cong\delta^{-1}\omega^{\otimes 2}
on
Y
, and that
Theorem
3.2.1
gives
{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})/{\mathcal{O}}}\cong\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}(\delta^{-1}\omega)
.
Recall also that the universal isogeny on
\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})
induces an isomorphism

\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}\widetilde{\delta}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}p^{f_{\mathfrak{p}}}\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}\widetilde{\delta}
which descends to
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})
,
so composing with multiplication by
p^{-f_{\mathfrak{p}}}
yields an isomorphism
\pi_{2}^{*}\delta\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\pi_{1}^{*}\delta
, or
equivalently
\pi_{1}^{*}\delta^{-1}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\pi_{2}^{*}\delta^{-1}
.
We may therefore view (
49
) as giving rise to an isomorphism

(52)

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})}&\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}&{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}(\pi_{1,*}(\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})}}\pi_{2}^{*}(\delta^{-1}\omega)),\,\delta^{-1}\omega^{\otimes 2})\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}&{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}(\pi_{1,*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\delta^{-1}\omega)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})}}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega),\,\delta^{-1}\omega^{\otimes 2})\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}&{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}(\delta^{-1}\omega\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}\pi_{1,*}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega,\,\delta^{-1}\omega\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}\omega)\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}&{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}(\pi_{1,*}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega,\,\omega),\end{array}

and we call the image of the unit section the
saving trace

(53)

{\operatorname{st}}:\,\pi_{1,*}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\longrightarrow\omega.

It follows from Proposition
3.2.3
and the definition of the saving trace that the
diagram

(54)

\textstyle{\pi_{1,*}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{st}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{1,*}\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}\,}_{\omega}}
\textstyle{\omega\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{f_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
\textstyle{\omega}

commutes, where the top arrow is the direct image of the descent of the morphism over
\widetilde{Y}_{U_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})}

induced by the universal isogeny.

More generally consider the morphisms
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}:Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{R}\to Y_{R}

for any (Noetherian)
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
R
(omitting the subscript
R
from the notation for the degeneracy maps).
It follows from Theorem
3.2.1
and Corollary
5.3.7
(for
{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})/{\mathcal{O}}}
and
i=0
)
that
(\pi_{1,*}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega)_{R}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\pi_{1,*}\pi_{2}^{*}(\omega_{R})

so the base-change of (
53
) defines a saving trace over
R
:

{\operatorname{st}}_{R}:\,\pi_{1,*}\pi_{2}^{*}(\omega_{R})\longrightarrow\omega_{R}.

Note also that Theorem
3.2.1
and Corollary
5.3.7
imply that
R^{i}\pi_{1,*}(\pi_{2}^{*}\omega_{R})\cong\omega_{R}^{-1}R^{i}\pi_{1,*}{\mathcal{K}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{R}/R}=0
for all
i>0
.
More generally, it follows that
if
{\mathcal{F}}
is any locally free sheaf on
Y_{R}
, then

R^{i}\pi_{1,*}(\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{R}}}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega_{R})\cong{\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{R}}}R^{i}\pi_{1,*}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega_{R}=0
for all
i>0
, and hence that

(55)

H^{i}(Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{R},\,\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{R}}}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega_{R})\cong H^{i}(Y_{R},\,{\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{R}}}\pi_{1,*}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega_{R}).

For
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
, we let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m}}
denote the line bundle
\bigotimes_{\theta}({\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}^{\otimes k_{\theta}}\otimes{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}^{\otimes m_{\theta}})
on
\widetilde{Y}
. Recall that if
\mu^{\mathbf{k}+2\mathbf{m}}:=\prod_{\theta}\theta(\mu)^{k_{\theta}+2m_{\theta}}

has trivial image in
R
for all
\mu\in U\cap{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{\times}
, then

\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R}
descends to
a line bundle on
Y_{R}
which we denote
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R}
.
We will now explain how to use the saving trace to construct a Hecke operator
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
on
H^{i}(Y_{R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})

for suitable
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
(and in particular whenever

k_{\theta}\geq 1
and
m_{\theta}\geq 0
for all
\theta
). Our main interest is in the case
i=0
and
F\neq\mathbb{Q}
,
in which case

M_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m}}(U;R):=H^{0}(Y_{R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})

is the space of Hilbert modular forms of weight
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m})
and level
U

with coefficients in
R
.

First recall that the universal isogeny
\psi:A_{1}\to A_{2}
over
\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})
induces morphisms

\psi_{\theta}^{*}:\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\to\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}
, and in turn
\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}\to\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}
and

\wedge^{2}\psi_{\theta}^{*}:\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}\to\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}

such that

\theta(\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}})\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}\subset\psi_{\theta}^{*}(\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta})\subset\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{\theta}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\wedge^{2}\psi_{\theta}^{*}(\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta})=\theta(\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}})\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}.

Since
v_{\mathcal{O}}(\theta(\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}))
is independent of
\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
and trivial for
\theta\not\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
, it follows that if

(56)

\sum_{\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\min\{m_{\theta},m_{\theta}+k_{\theta}-1\}\geq 0,

then
\psi_{\theta}^{*}
induces a morphism
\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{k}-\bf{1},\mathbf{m}}\to\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{1},\mathbf{m}}
.
Furthermore its base-change to
\widetilde{Y}_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{R}
descends to a morphism

(57)

{\pi}_{2}^{*}{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{1},\mathbf{m},R}\to{\pi}_{1}^{*}{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{1},\mathbf{m},R}

over
Y_{R}
.

For
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m})
satisfying (
56
) we define the endomorphism
T_{\mathfrak{p}}

of
H^{i}(Y_{R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})
as the composite

(58)

\begin{array}[]{rcl}H^{i}(Y_{R},\,{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})&\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}&H^{i}(Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{R},\,\pi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{1},\mathbf{m},R}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{R}}}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega_{R})\\
&{\longrightarrow}&H^{i}(Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{R},\,\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{1},\mathbf{m},R}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{R}}}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega_{R})\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}&H^{i}(Y_{R},\,{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{1},\mathbf{m},R}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{R}}}\pi_{1,*}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega_{R})\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}&H^{i}(Y_{R},\,{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R}),\end{array}

where the second arrow is induced by (
57
) and the third is (
55
).

It follows from the construction that the operator
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
is compatible with base-change, in the sense
that if
R\to R^{\prime}
is any homomorphism of
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras, then the resulting diagram

\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})\otimes_{R}R^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes 1}
\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{R^{\prime}},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{\mathfrak{p}}}
\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})\otimes_{R}R^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{R^{\prime}},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R^{\prime}})}

commutes. Furthermore it is straightforward to check that
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
is compatible with the action of

g\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
, in the sense that if
U
and
U^{\prime}
are sufficiently small and
g^{-1}Ug\subset U^{\prime}
,
then the resulting diagram

\textstyle{H^{i}(Y^{\prime}_{R},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{\mathfrak{p}}}
\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{R},\rho_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{\mathfrak{p}}}
\textstyle{H^{i}(Y^{\prime}_{R},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{R},\rho_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})}

commutes, where
\rho_{g}:Y_{R}\to Y^{\prime}_{R}:=Y_{U^{\prime},R}
and
\rho_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R}

are defined in §
2.3
. Finally the commutativity of (
54
) implies that
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
coincides
with the classical Hecke operator so denoted on the space
H^{0}(Y_{K},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},K})

of Hilbert modular forms of level
U
and weight
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m})
over
K
.

We record the result as follows:

Theorem 5.4.1
.

For sufficiently small open compact subgroups
U\subset\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F,\mathbf{f}})
containing

\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F,p})
, Noetherian
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras
R
, and
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
such that
\mu^{\mathbf{k}+2\mathbf{m}}
has
trivial image in
R
for all
\mu\in U\cap{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{\times}
and

\sum_{\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\min\{m_{\theta},m_{\theta}+k_{\theta}-1\}\geq 0,

the operators
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
defined on
H^{i}(Y_{U,R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})

by (
58
) are compatible with base-change and the action of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
,
and coincide with the classical Hecke operator
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
if
R
is a
K
-algebra and
i=0
.

Remark 5.4.2
.

The inequality (
56
) is needed to ensure integrality, but
results for more general
\mathbf{k}
,
\mathbf{m}
follow from twisting arguments. Indeed for fixed
\mathbf{k}

and varying
\mathbf{m}
, the modules
H^{i}(Y_{U,R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})
are isomorphic for
sufficiently small
U
. The isomorphisms are not canonical, but one can keep track of
its twisting effect on the action of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})
(see for example
[
DS23
, Lemma 4.6.1]

and
[
Dia23
, Prop. 3.2.2]
), and the same considerations apply to its effect on
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
In particular if
R
has characteristic zero (so
\mathbf{k}+2\mathbf{m}
is parallel), then we recover
the same integrality conditions as in
[
FP23
]
if
p
is unramified in
F
.

On the other hand if
R
has finite characteristic, then there is no restriction on
\mathbf{k}
and
\mathbf{m}
,
and the critical case for
i=0
becomes
\mathbf{m}=\mathbf{0}
. Note that the hypothesis (
56
)
is then equivalent to
k_{\theta}\geq 1
for all
\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
, which is known to hold in the
cases of primary interest thanks to the main result of
[
DK23
]
and
[
DDW24
, Prop. 1.13]
(see also
[
Dia23
, Thm. D]
).

Note also that if
\mathbf{m}=\mathbf{0}
, then the inequality (
56
) becomes an equality. By contrast
if the inequality is strict one finds that
T_{\mathfrak{p}}=0
if
R
is a
k
-algebra, and more generally
that
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
is nilpotent if
p^{n}R=0
.

Remark 5.4.3
.

Interchanging the roles of
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
, one can similarly define a saving trace

{\operatorname{st}}^{\prime}:\pi_{2,*}\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\to\omega
(without using the isomorphism
\pi_{2}^{*}\delta\cong\pi_{1}^{*}\delta
). A construction
analogous to the one above then defines an operator
T_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
on
H^{i}(Y_{R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})
whenever

\sum_{\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\max\{m_{\theta},m_{\theta}+k_{\theta}-1\}\leq 0
,
and the analogue of the commutativity of (
54
) is then that of the diagram

(59)

\textstyle{\pi_{2,*}\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}\,}^{\prime}_{\omega}}
\textstyle{\pi_{2,*}\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{st}}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\omega,}

where
{\operatorname{tr}\,}^{\prime}_{\omega}
extends the trace relative to
\pi_{2,K}
.

Note that if
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{1}
and
\mathbf{m}=\mathbf{0}

(or more generally
k_{\theta}=1
for all
\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\sum_{\theta\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}m_{\theta}=0
), then
both
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
T_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
are defined. Using the commutativity of (
54
) and (
59
),
it is straightforward to check that
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the composite of
T_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
with the
automorphism of
H^{i}(Y_{R},\omega_{R})
induced by the map
\sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}:Y\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}{Y}
obtained by descent
from
\underline{A}\mapsto\underline{A}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}
, together with the isomorphism

\sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}^{*}{\omega}=\mathrm{Nm}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\omega}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\omega
defined by
p^{-f_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

Remark 5.4.4
.

The main results of this paper, in particular Theorem 3.2.1 and Theorem 5.3.1 (and its corollaries), are extended in
[
Dia24
, §5]
to toroidal compactifications. Furthermore the effect of the saving trace on
q
-expansions is given by Proposition 5.3.1 of
[
Dia24
]
, leading to that of
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
in Proposition 6.8.1. In particular this implies the commutativity of the Hecke operators
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
(whenever defined) on
H^{0}(Y_{R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})
for varying
{\mathfrak{p}}
in
S_{p}
.

By contrast, checking this directly from their construction leads to a formidable diagram whose commutativity ultimately
seems to require analogues of some of the results in
§
5.3
for direct images under the projections
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime})\to Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime})
.
While these might follow from arguments along similar lines to those for direct images under
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})\to Y
or from their compatibility with more general base-changes than we considered (more precisely, with respect to
Y_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})
instead of
{\mathcal{O}}
), we have not carried these out. Consequently, we have not shown the commutativity of the operators
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
on
H^{i}(Y_{R},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{m},R})
for
i>0
.
